MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-01-13 | CLIP_ID: 409 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 409

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 January 13, 2014
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: MAYOR - Richard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and
Ms. Williams.  Absent: Mr. Allen.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Dan White, City
Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Recognition was made and congratulations extended to Michael Martinez-Montano, Cassidy
Weber, Elissa Pino and Kiahna Pisano for being selected as the Cheyenne Boys and Girls Club Youth
of the Year.  It was noted that Ms. Pisano will be Wyoming’s representative at the national level.
ELECTION OF CITY COUNCIL PRESIDENT FOR 2014.  Mayor Kaysen noted for the
record that, pursuant to City Code, he would be conducting the election for the office of City Council
President but would not be voting, and advised nominations were in order.  Mr. Shanor moved to
nominate and elect Dr. Rinne as City Council President for 2014, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  There
being no further nominations, Mr. Shanor moved that nominations cease, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of
Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.  Comment followed in support of the nominee.  Motion to nominate
and elect Dr. Rinne carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the
exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.
ELECTION OF CITY COUNCIL VICE-PRESIDENT FOR 2014.  Mayor Kaysen noted for
the record that, pursuant to City Code, he would be conducting the election for the office of City
Council Vice-President but would not be voting, and advised nominations were in order.  Ms. Broyles
moved to nominate and elect Mr. Allen as City Council Vice-President for 2014, seconded by Dr.
Rinne.  There being no further nominations, Mr. Shanor moved that nominations cease, seconded by
Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body present with the
exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.  Motion to nom inate and e lect Mr. Allen c arried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who
did not vote.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member  of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Shanor, and pursuant to support received by
Ms. Broyles and Dr. Rinne, Agenda Item #15 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Shanor
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 23, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers
payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services and Radio Station KYOY-F M respectively) and vacated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by
Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council
Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez returned to Council
Chambers.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from NB Neighborhood Business to NR-2 Neighborhood
Residential – Medium Densit y for L ots 15 and 16, Bloc k 17, Crest Ridge , 4  Filing, Cheyenne,th
Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to Storey Boulevard, between Canyon and Ridge Roads).
Roger Alexander, a djacent property  owner , spoke in ag reement with the propose d zoning
classification but expressed concerns with proposed development plans for the property relative to
such topics as buff ering, scre ening and lig hting, a nd the deve lopment being inconsistent and
incompatible with the existing neighborhood.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Discussion followed on zoning
criteria being met by  the deve loper, and the possibilit y to addre ss additional development plan
concerns, which would include  the opportunity  for public input, as the pr oject proceeds.  U pon
inquiry, Brandon Cammarata, Planning and Development Director, advised that options available that
have been discussed may involve either a conditional use approval or plat approval process.  Upon
further inquiry, Mayor Kaysen confirmed if a plat came before the governing body the process would
provide for public input.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
(#4014)
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from MR-1 Medium Density Residential – Established and
MUR Mixed Use Residential Emphasis to MUR Mixed Use Residential Emphasis for the N1/2 of
Lot 2, and all of Lots 3 & 4, Block 236, Original City (to be known as Keefe Cottages Subdivision),
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of the intersection of Evans Avenue and E. 22nd
Street).  Mr. Shanor  moved to approve  on third and final r eading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Upon
inquiry, Brandon Cammarata, Planning and Development Director, stated it was his understanding
the applicant is seeking the zone change as a housekeeping effort since most of the property is already
zoned MUR, and the change will provide for continuation of existing uses (detached garages) and
that he wa s not awar e of a ny properties within the block being  used for  a commer cial purpose.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#4015)
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from MR-1 Medium Density Residential-Established to
MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis for Lot 1, and the East 34-feet of Lot 2, Block 382, Original
City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the intersection of Pebrican Avenue
and E. 17  Street).  Jan Nelson-Schroll expressed concerns with the MUB zone classification relativeth
to allowing more business activities within the residential neighborhood.  She suggested a policy be
put into place whe reby a house, or  the pr operty site, could be e asily transitioned bac k into a
residentially zoned use.  She commented in support of pr eservation efforts concerning proposed
renovation of the house located upon the property.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on third and final
reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Discussion followed on concerns with commercial developments
and ventur es enc roaching into re sidential are as, and w hether there  would be any  problem
procedurally if, in the future, use of the  proper ty wer e to re turn to residential use.  Jonathan
Woodward, Development Office, advised that a residential use is permitted within a MUB zone so
a zone change action would not be required if a property owner wanted to change the business use
back to residential use.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present
with the exception of Dr. Rinne and Ms. Williams voting “no”. (#4016)
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code changing the zoning classification from LI Light Industrial to MUR Mixed-Use Residential
Emphasis for Lot 8, Block 223, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northwest corner
of the intersection of Dillon Avenue and W. 21  Street) -- referred to Public Services Committeest
(sponsor -- Mr. Allen).
ORDINANCE - Vacating a portion of 16 foot alley in Block 353, Original City, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of the intersection of W. 18  Street and Thomes Avenue) --th
referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor -- Mr. Allen). 
[CA]  RESOLUTION - Designating local banks as depositories for municipal funds.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried. (#5557)
RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a  Final Plat for K eefe
Cottages Subdivision, a replat of the North ½ of Lot 2, and all of Lots 3 and 4, Block 236, Original
City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of the intersection of Evans Avenue and
E. 22nd Street).  Casey  Palma, Steil S urveying Ser vices, a gent for the  applicants, provide d
information regarding the affiliated zone change acted upon earlier in the meeting, noting the r e-
zoning of the pr operty provides for  retaining existing uses of the pr operty, and advise d the plat
includes designation of separate lots for areas currently having garage structures and provides for an
access easement to a shared alley.  During discussion and upon inquiry, Mr. Palma stated the lots are
small in size and any parking area for items such as boats and rec reational vehicles would be no
different than other  properties; that no additional traff ic volume due to the platting  action wa s
foreseen, and that curr ently there are no construction plans involving  the prope rty.  Mr. Shanor
moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Following brief discussion, motion carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body present. (#5558)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Approving the five year Transit Development Plan for the Cheyenne Transit
Program as a flexible planning tool for future improvements to the Cheyenne area transit program.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5559)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to grant an easement to the United States
of America, Department of Energy, Western Area Power Administration, for the installation of buried
fiber optic and other communications cables within Western Area Power Administration’s existing
electric transmission line easement over a parcel of land owned by the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5560)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to execute a S tate Hi storic
Preservation Office Certified Local Government Grant Agreement for attendance at the 2014 Saving
Places Conference -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor -- Mr. Allen). 
[CA] RESOLUTION - D esignating the Sunday s during 2014 th at annual liquor licensee s may
extend dispensing room hours of operation for sales until 2:00 A.M. the following day -- referred to
Finance Committee (sponsor -- Mr. Brown).
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and Stride Learning Center for
use of a 2006 Chevrolet El Dorado bus for services for Stride clients.  Motion (per Consent Agenda)
carried. (#6118)
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Frontier Refining, LLC, designating Holly
Frontier Refining Corporation as the title sponsor of the annual Superday event for 2014, 2015 and
2016.  Motion (per Consent Ag enda) carried (revenue to the City  in the amount of $25,000.00
annually). (#6119)
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City  of Chey enne, Police D epartment, and
Laramie County  Sheriff’s Depa rtment for Explosive Ordnanc e and D isposal (EOD) , Special
Weapons and Tactics (SWAT) and Crisis Negotiation Unit (CNU) operations and law enforcement
extraterritorial assistance and activities -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 5965 between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and
Recreation Depa rtment, and DH M Desig n Corporation for  additional desig n s ervices for  the
Converse So ftball Co mplex Re novation p roject ( Menard p roperty s ale p roceeds).  M otion ( per
Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $7,400.00). (#5965)
[CA] Authorization to purchase tra ffic striping paint and beads under Wyoming Department of
Transportation Bid #14-002TZ and #14-003TZ (2011-2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
[CA] Consideration of B id #R-8-14 for f urnishing tw o new 2014-2015 trucks for the City  of
Cheyenne, Public Works Department (2011 - 2014 1% Sales Tax & General Small Equipment fund)
– referred to Finance Committee. 
Announcement was made  of the Re- Appointments of Ann Picot and Dar yl Hensel to the
Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities, to be confirmed January 27, 2014.
Announcement was made that due to the Martin Luther King, Jr. Holiday on Monday, January
20, 2014, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the Municipal
Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 122, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance Committee
will meet Tuesday, J anuary 21, 2014 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Service s Commi ttee will meet
Wednesday, January 22, 2014 at Noon.
Under other business:
Ms. Broyles st ated th at at  the  December 2 3, 20 13 City Cou ncil m eeting, th e mot ion to
approve on 3  and fina l rea ding the  ordinanc e involving sig n amendments to the Unifie drd
Development Code failed; that she had voted on the prevailing side and, pursuant to Chapter 2.04 of
City Code, moved to reconsider the vote taken on the ordinance on 3  and final reading, secondedrd
by Mr. Cook.  Upon request by Mayor Kaysen, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, read the entitlement of
the ordinance proposed for reconsideration into the record as follows: An Ordinance amending the
Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC), as approved by Ordinance No. 3943 on January 23,
2012, to addre ss issues raised re garding City sign r egulations.  Ms. Broy les commented on the
purpose of her motion to reconsider the ordinance.  Motion to reconsider carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the g overning body present.  Ms. Broy les moved to amend by  substitute ordinance,
seconded by Mr. Cook.  Comments followed on the proposed substitute addressing election/political
signs as a separate issue at this time, and concern that the proposed substitute does not adequately
address constitutional issues present in t he current ordinance.  Motion to amend c arried.  Voting
“yes” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” – Mr. Luna,
Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Ms. Broyles moved to postpone the item to January 27,
2014, seconded by Mr. Brown.  It was noted postponement will provide the opportunity for the public
to be awa re of  the re consideration action and provide  input on the substit ute ordina nce be ing
proposed.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
Mayor Kaysen advised the item will be reviewed for recommendation at the next Public Services
Committee meeting, which will be held Wednesday, January 22, 2014 at Noon in Room 122,
Municipal Building, due to the Martin Luther King, Jr. Holiday that week.
Upon inquiry, Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, advised staff is looking at options
with regard to swimming f acilities that may  be ava ilable to the public during  the closure  of the
Municipal Pool in Lions Park due to construction beginning this spring.
It was noted the annual Martin Luther King, Jr. dinner banquet will be held at the Holiday Inn
on January 19, 2014 beginning at 6:00 p.m., and the annual public march (from the Depot to Capitol
Building) will be held on January 20, 2014 in observance of the holiday.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 6:49 p.m. 
 Submitted by,
  
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 January 27, 2014
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: MAYOR - Richard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr.
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Dan White, City Attorney.
The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member  of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Shanor  moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 13, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Service s) and va cated Council Chambers.  Mr. Va ldez moved  to approve  pay ment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to E/S3
Consultants, Inc., Jim Elias, Public Works Director, clarified the consultant is finishing up work on
the City’s energy efficiency project with regard to federal Buy America provisions.  Motion carried.
Voting “ yes” - - all member s of the g overning body prese nt in Council C hambers.  Following
announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
ORDINANCE - A mending the Ch eyenne Unified Development (UDC), as appr oved by
Ordinance No . 3 943 o n J anuary 2 3, 2 012, t o ad opt am endments i dentified as  el ection s ign
amendments from the 2013 annual review (postponed from Jan. 13, 2014).  Reporting for Public
Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.
Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to Feb. 10, 2014, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry, Dan White,
City Attorney, advised in view of  the lawsuit that has bee n filed ag ainst the City , outside leg al
counsel has been retained to review necessary pleadings and to offer advice on proposed ordinance
wording.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Mayor
Kaysen noted the item will be reviewed at the next Public Services Committee to be held on Feb. 4,
2014 at noon in Room 122, Municipal Building.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code changing the zoning classification from LI Light Industrial to MUR Mixed-Use Residential
Emphasis for Lot 8, Block 223, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northwest corner
of the intersection of Dillon Avenue and W. 21  Street).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on secondst
reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  U pon inquiry regarding non-conforming uses in the area, Brett
Walker, Development Office, stated it would probably be beneficial to have a small area plan in place
for future considerations and to include options that may be available based upon zoning requests
from other property owners in the area.  Comments followed on the purpose of re-zoning the area to
Light Industrial (LI) several years ago, and the need to re-evaluate area zoning relative to residential
uses.  Dan Edeen, Phoenix Builders, advised he was in attendance to answer any questions.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Vacating a portion of 16 foot alley in Block 353, Original City, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of the intersection of W. 18  Street and Thomes Avenue).th
Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  It was noted that a portion
of the alley is being requested to be vacated in preparation for construction of the new public safety
building.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
ORDINANCE - A mending the Chey enne U nified De velopment Code (U DC), to adopt
amendments to increase the maximum Administrative Adjustment from 10% to 50% for infill and
redevelopment as initiated from the 2013 annual review -- referred to Public Services Committee
(sponsor -- Mr. Shanor).
 
ORDINANCE - Amending various sections of the Chey enne Unified Development Code
(UDC), to add an Urban Loft Lot Type (NB3) and “Urban Loft Building” definition, allowed as a Use
Permitted By Right and Use Permitted after Conditional Discretionary Review in Residential, Mixed
Use and Neighborhood Business districts -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor -- Mr.
Shanor).
[CA] RESOLUTION - A uthorizing the M ayor and the City Clerk to e xecute a State Historic
Preservation Office Certified Local Government Grant Agreement for attendance at the 2014 Saving
Places Conference.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5561 Res./#6122 Contract)
 
RESOLUTION - Des ignating the Sunday s during 2014 that annual liquor lice nsees may
extend dispensing room hours of operation for sales until 2:00 A.M. the following day.  K evin
Georges, President, Laramie County Liquor Dealers Association, advised the requested Sundays
(Super Bowl Sunday, Sunday of Memorial Day weekend, 1  Sunday of Frontier Days, and Sundayst
of Labor Day weekend) were chosen based on time frames involving special events and activities and
provides licensees with a choice of extending hours for sales to promote patronage of benefit to
businesses and in support of tourism.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to adopt,
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Upon inquiry, Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, provided information
relative to the depa rtment’s report on violations/offenses oc curring during past years’ approved
Sundays, noting the total number of offenses in the report may not have involved alcohol.  Comment
followed on state legislative vs. local government involvement in addressing extended hours, and
concern with alcohol-based incidents and related vehicular accidents in general.  Motion carrie d.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Broyles voting “no”.
(#5562)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing submission of a Mineral Royalty Capital Construction Account
Grant (Ch apter 3) application to the State L oan and I nvestment Board for the Tra nsfer Station
Upgrade Phase II -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsors -- Mr. Brown and Mr. Allen).
[CA] RESOLUTION - Setting aside monies in the 2015 fiscal year budget to be used to implement
corrective or  emer gency measures  for structures that are  deeme d vaca nt, unsafe, da ngerous or
otherwise unfit for human occupancy pursuant to the City of Cheyenne Dangerous Building Code --
referred to Finance Committee (sponsor -- Ms. Williams).
[CA] RESOLUTION - A uthorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a  Final Plat for  the
Cheyenne Public Safety Center, a replat of portions of Lots 1 thru 8, and that portion of vacated alley,
Block 353, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of the intersection of
W. 18 th Street and Thomes Avenue) -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor --  Mr.
Shanor). 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City  of Chey enne, Police Dep artment, and
Laramie County  Sheriff’ s Depa rtment for Explosive Ordnanc e an d Disposal (EOD) , Special
Weapons and Tactics (SWAT) and Crisis Negotiation Unit (CNU) operations and law enforcement
extraterritorial assistance and activities.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6121) 
[CA] Amendment No. 1 to Professional Services Contract No. 5917 between the City of Cheyenne,
Metropolitan Planning Organization, and Ayres Associates, Inc. for the Dell Range Corridor Study,
Phase II (Federal MPO funds, Federal Highway Safety Grant) -- referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-8-14 for furnishing two new 2014-2015 trucks for  the City  of
Cheyenne, Public Works Department (2011 - 2014 1% Sales Tax & General Small Equipment fund).
Motion (per Consent Ag enda) carried (accept bid from Spradley  Barr Motors, Chey enne, in a n
amount not to exceed $117,736.61).
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-12-14 for constructing a wood structure, metal siding building,
(pole barn) for the City  of Chey enne, Parks & Recreation Department, Urban Forestry Division
(2011-2014 1%  Sa les Tax & C ontractual Maintenance Account Urban Forestry) -- referred to
Finance Committee. 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne Public Works Department,
Transit Division an d Radio Engineering Industries, Inc., to provide Computer A ided Scheduling
Software with Mobile Data Terminals (Transit Federal funds) -- referred to Finance Committee.  
Re-Appointments of Ann Picot and Daryl Hensel to the Mayor’s Council for People with
Disabilities.  Ms. Broyles moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral
appointments).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 10, 2014, at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a transfer of ownership for
a retail  liquor lice nse a pplication f iled w ith the  City  Cle rk’s Of fice fo r M inglemill, I nc. d /b/a
Mingles, 1618 Stillwater Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held by Stillwater, Inc.
d/b/a Mingles, 1618 Stillwater Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming). 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 6:25 p.m. 
 Submitted by,

 City Clerk

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 February 10, 2014
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: MAYOR - Richard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr.
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and
Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member  of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Luna moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded by
Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 27, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Service s) and va cated Council Chambers.  Mr. Va ldez moved  to approve  pay ment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Pursuant to inquiry regarding a voucher payable to
EQ Sound for wireless microphones used for a Civic Center production and whether there is a local
company that can provide the service, Mayor Kaysen advised he would contact applicable staff and
provide follow-up information.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to Council
Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a transfer of
ownership for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Minglemill, Inc.
d/b/a Mingles, 1618 Stillwater Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held by Stillwater,
Inc. d/b/a Mingles, 1618 Stillwater Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming).  There being no comments or
input and  purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.  The
item was referred to Finance Committee.
ORDINANCE - Amending the Cheyenne Unified Development (UDC), as approved by
Ordinance No . 3 943 o n J anuary 2 3, 2 012, t o ad opt am endments i dentified as  el ection s ign
amendments from the 2013 annual review (postponed from Jan. 27, 2014).  Reporting for Public
Services Committee, Mr. Shanor moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.
Mr. Shanor moved to amend by substitute dated 02-04-14, seconded by Mr. Allen.  (City Clerk note -
title of substitute ordinance:  An Ordinance, pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Chey enne
Unified Development Code (“UDC”), amending Section 6.5.5.b. to add exemptions from the sign
permit requirements of Article 6.5 of the UDC relating to signs.)  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City
Attorney, clarified the purpose of the amendment is to exempt from the sign regulations of the UDC
those types of signs that are both easily placed and removed from the ground, without structures,
construction or specialized equipment, and which relate to candidates, issues, propositions or other
matters to be voted upon by the electors of the city, or which relate to p hilosophical, religious,
political, charitable or other similar noncommercial messages.  M. Lee Hasenauer, Laramie County
Commissioner, commented in support of addressing the constitutional issue and spoke in favor of
the amendment.  Upon inquiry for clarification, Mr. White confirmed the effect of the amendment
would be that political and ideological signs would have no restriction on the amount of time they
can be posted, or the size or number of signs that can be placed on a lot.  Discussion topics included:
Concerns with having unrestricted signs, such as sign size/total size; objectives of the sign ordinance
to maintain and enhance visual qualities and aesthetics to the community and promote pedestrian and
vehicle sa fety b y minimizing distra ctions, and addr essing reg ulations, if neede d, in the future.
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez,
Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne.  Comments
followed regarding concerns that had been raised affiliated with non-regulation, including sign size,
number a nd setbac ks, and re ctifying the  constit utional issue throug h the proposed a mended
ordinance.  Ms. Broyles moved to postpone for two weeks.  Motion died due to the lack of a second.
Main motion on third and final reading, as amended by substitute, carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen,
Mr. Brown, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez, Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” --
Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne. (#4017)
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code changing the zoning classification from LI Light Industrial to MUR Mixed-Use Residential
Emphasis for Lot 8, Block 223, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northwest corner
of the intersection of Dillon Avenue and W. 21  Street).  Dan Edeen, Phoenix Builders, representingst
the property owners, briefly reviewed the purpose of the zone change, which is to allow the owners
to build a new residence on the site that will be up to codes.  Jan Nelson-Schroll spoke in support of
the area being zoned residential and not light industrial, and expressed concerns that there is nothing
in place through the City to help people restore older, antique homes in lieu of tearing them down and
rebuilding.  Boyd Wiggam, Wyoming Liberty Group, commented in support of the zone cha nge,
expressing that the property owner’s situation reflects a broader, wide-spread problem with zoning
classifications vs. uses on the west side of the City that should be addressed.  Mr. Shanor moved to
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Following brief comment, motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#4018)
ORDINANCE - Vacating a portion of 16 foot alley in Block 353, Original City, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of the intersection of W. 18  Street and Thomes Avenue).th
Mr. Shanor moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#4019) 
ORDINANCE - A mending the Chey enne U nified De velopment Code (UDC), to adopt
amendments to increase the maximum Administrative Adjustment from 10% to 50% for infill and
redevelopment as initiated from the 2013 annual review.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second
reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by substitute ordinance, seconded by
Mr. Allen. I t was advised the  purpose of  the substitute is t o include that an e xplanation to the
applicant and the public will be provided if the administrative adjustment is approved, and to include
a sunset  provision to determine effectiveness of the ordinance after one year.  D iscussion topics
included:  D ecision of the  director relative to a n applicant appeal process; consideration that a
notification process to residents in the area involved with the application be included; purpose and
benefit of including a sunset clause; amending the current administrative adjustment percentage to
assisting in spurring re-development; annual UDC review requirement, and responsibility of notifying
City Council members when the ordinance will sunset.  Upon query to City Council members by
Mayor Kaysen to allow him to voice an observation regarding the sunset clause, and there being no
objection raised, Mayor Kaysen noted that the a nnual UDC re view process may take c are of the
purpose of including a sunset clause.  Mayor Kaysen advised that administration staff will provide
notification to the governing body when the ordinance would “sunset” if the clause is included in the
final ordinanc e.  Motion to amend by  substitut e ca rried.  Voting “yes” - - all member s of the
governing body.  Mr. Allen, sec onded by Dr. Rinne, m oved to amend the substitute ordinanc e as
follows: Strike the words “is final and” in Section 6 under Specific Procedures so the last sentence
would instead read “The decision of the Director shall be given to the applicant in writing, and a copy
shall be made available for public review.”; change numbering of the next section from Section 6 to
Section 7, and in the newly re-numbered Section 7 insert the word “hereof” at the end of the first
sentence and strike the last sentence in its entirety.  It was verified that the proposed changes to
Section 7 would result in the section reading as follows: “That this ordinance shall be in full force
and effect upon its approval and publication for a period of one year hereof.”  It was noted that the
amendment is to incorporate some suggestions offered by the City Attorney.  Motion to amend the
substitute carried.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the g overning body.  Discussion followed on
possible future amendment that may be proposed at the next Public Services Committee meeting with
regard to a neighborhood notification requirement; purpose of the ordinance, which is to assist with
re-development of existing properties; sunset clause to allow for review by the governing body, and
notification requirements (signage) affiliated with the site plan process.  Main motion to approve as
twice amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
 
ORDINANCE - Amending various sections of the Chey enne Unified Development Code

(CBD) zone.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.

[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing submission of a Mineral Royalty Capital Construction Account
Grant (Chapter 3) application to the State Loan and Investment Board for the Transfer S tation
Upgrade Phase II.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5563)
RESOLUTION - Setting aside monies in the 2015 fiscal year budget to be used to implement
corrective or  emer gency measures f or structure s that are  deeme d vaca nt, unsafe, da ngerous or
otherwise unfit for human occupancy pursuant to the City of Cheyenne Dangerous Building Code.
Mr. Shanor recused himself from discussion and voting on the item to avoid the appearance of any
potential or perceived conflict of interest, and vacated Council Chambers.  Pete Laybourn spoke in
support of the resolution, referencing a past ordinance that applied to abandoned residential structures
which was no longer in effect due to a sunset expiration clause.  Mr. Laybourn expressed concerns
with vacant structures and community safety, and voiced support for providing budget funding to
assist w ith c ode e nforcement.  Reporting fo r Finance Com mittee, M r. Brown mov ed to  a dopt,
seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mr. Brown moved to amend to increase the funding amount to $25,000,
seconded by Ms. Williams.  Boyd Wiggam, Wyoming Liberty Group, expressed his understanding
that the proposed inc rease in funding  would primarily  be used to c over costs of filing liens a nd
litigation if affected properties resulted in transferring private property into public ownership.  He
requested the initial $5,000 funding request be kept for use to board up buildings, and that results of
re-development and infill projects and initiatives be reviewed for effectiveness before increasing the
funding.  Upon inquiry, discussion followed regarding the rationale for increasing the funding request
from $5,000 to $25,000 and potential funding sources. During discussion, Mr. Brown raised a point
of order that comments being made did not pertain to wording of the resolution with Mayor Kaysen
ruling that comments could continue.  D iscussion continued and included the following topics:
Potential funding sour ces, including g rants; available City Council continge ncy funds; estimated
number of properties containing abandoned or vacant houses/structures that may be involved per
Council Ward; code enforcement; upcoming budget process for next fiscal year and concern with the
increased funding amount; dangerous buildings as a community health, safety and welfare issue, and
status of curr ent budget revenues vs. expenditures.  During  discussion reg arding the number of
estimated properties that may be involved, Mr. Allen raised a point of order that comments were not
germane to t he motion on the floor with Mayor Kaysen concurring and advising that comments
should stay on topic.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Broyles and Ms.
Williams.  Voting “no” --  Mr. B rown, Mr. Cook, Mr. L una, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valde z and May or
Kaysen.  Ms. Broyles moved to amend by adding the amount of $2,040.49 from Council Ward III
contingency funds to the $5,000 funding request, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Dr. Rinne raised a point
of information regarding the proc ess that has be en utilized by Council members on de termining
approval f or us e of  Cou ncil c ontingency fu nds p er Ward .  Mayor Ka ysen a dvised it  wa s h is
understanding that past practice has been that approval of Council contingency funds are determined
by City Council members who represent each Ward.  Discussion followed on abstention from voting
on use of contingency funds by Council members who do not represent the Ward involved in the
motion to amend and affiliated concerns, and history of Council contingency funds per Ward and
approval for use.  M ayor Kaysen recognized Mr. Laybourn, who provided information on past
disbursement of Council Ward I  contingency funds throug h a re solution process, which wa s the
preference of Council Ward I members at that time when he served on C ity Council.  Motion to
amend failed.  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Broyles and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown,
Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Pursuant to comments regarding
recouping expenses through a lien process and costs, and upon inquiry, Bruce Wilson, Chief Building
Official, reviewed generally the process that is followed relative to notices and orders that are issued
to property owners and further actions if non-compliance continues, including boarding up/securing
of properties.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. Wilson explained the past ordinance, which is no longer in
effect, required owners to register abandoned residential buildings and that of all of the properties
registered only one was truly abandoned with others involving properties that had gone through
foreclosure by mortg age companies.  Upon inquiry , May or Ka ysen stated the c ost for aba ting
nuisances is attached to the pr operty owner and sometimes collec tion efforts are suc cessful and
sometimes not.  Upon query, Mr. Wilson stated it would cost approximately $150-$300 to board up
a house, depending on the number of openings.  Discussion continued and included banks owning
properties that may be abandoned or vacant; identification of properties and lack of communications
between various agencies to assist with identification.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote,
Mr. Shanor returned to Council Chambers. (#5564)
RESOLUTION - A uthorizing the May or and the City Clerk to sig n a F inal Plat for  the

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-02-24 | CLIP_ID: 415 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 415

  RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 February 24, 2014
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: MAYOR - Richard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr.
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and
Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing  body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member  of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by
Dr. Rinne and Ms. B royles, Agenda Item #20(c) was r emoved from the Consent Ag enda.  Upon
request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Ms. Broyles and Mr. Shanor, Agenda Item
#23(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Ms. Broyles moved to approve remaining Consent
Agenda items, sec onded by Ms. Wi lliams.  Moti on carried.  Voting  “yes” -- all members of  the
governing body.  Mayor Kaysen advised that Agenda Item #19 (Resolution Adopting PlanCheyenne:
Reflections and Progress) on the Consent Agenda that was referred to Committee of the Whole would
be reviewed and discussed at the Committee meeting, open to the public for comments, scheduled
to be held Tuesday, March 4, 2014 at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 10, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Service s) and va cated Council Chambers.  Mr.  Valdez moved to appr ove pay ment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to Tatanka
Historical Associates Inc., Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, advised the vouc her was for
services involving historic structure analysis required by SHPO (State Historic Preservation Office),
affiliated with utilization of CDBG (Community Development Block Grant) funding, for the West
Edge Project area.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in
Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a Public Hearing to receive comments and input for renewal of existing
City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, and microbrewery
permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  Pursuant to inquiry regarding the police chief’s annual
report concerning compliance checks involving on-site attempted purchases of alcohol by underage
individuals through the Department’s compliance program, Detective John Gay, Cheyenne Police
Department and Alcohol Compliance Officer, provided information regarding violations relative to
certain licensee s; warr ants and summonses that had be en i ssued for  the re porting time frame ;
summons pr ocedures; how compliance  inspections are  conducte d, and g eneral proc edures and
assistance to licensees regarding corrective measures.  Comments followed regarding TIPS training
and servers being allowed to serve alcohol before they have gone through the training and addressing
the issue through state legislation or by City Council review and action.  Kevin Beers, Cadillac Ranch
Bar owner, reviewed TIPS training time frames for his staff and a reward program he offers to
patrons for notification of underage individuals in the bar.  Bryan Grzegorczyk, Alf’s Pub and
Package Liquor, discussed kee ping the community safe and questioned what c onstitutes a sa les
“transaction” with regard to attempted alcohol pur chases by underage individuals resulting  in a
written citation being issued.  Chief B rian Kozak, Police Department, clarified a violation occurs
when the a lcohol is  delivered or ser ved to the under age individual and timing of  the monetar y
exchange for the sale does not matter.  Comments continued on those licensees that had received
compliance check violations and procedures before the governing body that are implemented after
a third violation within a year.  Upon inquiry, Chief Kozak clarified that pursuant to City Code the
Chief provides notification to City Council if a third violation occurs, and noted that those licensees
who have received two violations as noted in the report will have follow-up compliance checks.
Among comments expressed was concern that parents are taking underage children into package
stores within the City  in violation of City Code.  The re being no f urther comments or input and
purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the
applications to Finance Committee.  He noted the Committee will meet to review the applications
on Monday, March 3, 2014 in Room 122, Municipal Building, at 11:00 a.m.
ORDINANCE - A mending t he Cheyenne Unified De velopment Code (UD C), to adopt
amendments to increase the maximum Administrative Adjustment from 10% to 50% for infill and
redevelopment as initiated from the 2013 annual review.  Albert Simpson expressed his concern with
reducing standards and suggested the authority be left to the landowner  on what they  want to do
within the 50% limit.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor moved to approve on
third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Mr. Shanor, seconded by Ms. Broyles, moved
to amend by substitute ordinance by renumbering the current Section 7 to 8, and inserting a  new
Section 7 with the following language:  “In cases where more than 25% of administrative adjustment
is exercised, the developer will be required to notify all property owners within 140 feet of the subject
area boundaries, excluding rights-of-way.”.  B oyd Wiggam, Wyoming Liberty Group, spoke in
opposition to the amendment, commenting on the current process involving sign notification placed
on the property, that the amendment potentially promotes neighbor activism, and the need to protect
use of property for owners.  Jim Weaver, #1 Properties and commercial real estate broker, questioned
the intent and purpose of the proposed mailing notice requirement by the developer, and what the
next process would be by  staff if r esponses are received as a  result of the notice .  I n response,
Brandon Camm arata, Planning and De velopment Dire ctor, e xpressed that the mailed notice
requirement may seem disproportionate to the minor adjustments being attempted to be addressed;
that the notice may  increase approval times, and that ther e is nothing spe cified regarding a staff
and/or Council process relative to responses being received as a result of the notices.  Jan Nelson-
Schroll commented on property and neighborhood aesthetics, and balancing the rights and interests
of developing property owners and those of neighboring owners.  Comments followed on the intent
of the proposed amendment involving mailed notices; provisions of Wyo. Statute Section 15-1-603,
and wording contained in UDC Section 2.3.4 b. 3.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised
it was his understanding that the 140 ft. notification referenced in state statute concerns zone changes,
and commented that the purpose (i.e. ordinance topic generally) is a less significant change, dealing
with an administrative change during a site plan process.  M otion to amend by substitute failed.
Voting “yes” -- Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr.
Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend Section
7 by replacing the 12 month time period with 18 months, seconded by  Mr. Allen.  Explanation
followed on extending the ordinance “sunset” time period to allow for coordination with the annual
UDC review process with public input.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the g overning body.  Main motion , as amended, carried.  Voting  “yes” - - all member s of the
governing body. (#4020)
 
ORDINANCE - Amending various sections of the Chey enne Unified Development Code
(UDC), to add an Urban Loft Lot Type (NB3) and “Urban Loft Building” definition, allowed as a Use
Permitted By Right and Use Permitted after Conditional Discretionary Review in Residential, Mixed
Use and Neighborhood Business districts.  Albert Simpson expressed objection to the governing body
establishing  maximum setbacks, noting this should be determined by the property owner, and stated
his observation that a parking spot should be required for every bedroom affiliated with a project.
Mr. Shanor moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Mr. Shanor moved
to amend Table 5-1 by adding “C”-Use permitted after conditional discretionary review in the CBD
column for Urban Loft Building, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body.  Main motion, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”. (#4021)
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, c hanging the  zon ing c lassification f rom CB  Com munity B usiness to MU R Mi xed-Use
Residential Emphasis for the south 79 feet of Lot 8, Block 311, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming
(located at the northwest corner of the intersection of E. 18  Street and Logan Avenue).th   Mr. Shanor
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body.  
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to LI Light Industrial for Lot
1, Block 1, Public Works Addition, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located on the west side of North College
Drive).  Jean Vetter, High Plains Design, agent for the applicant, provided a brief description of the
project and area involved (i.e. Transfer Station), advising the zone change will allow for conformance
with use of the property.  Ms. Vetter explained the types of hazardous household wastes that will be
collected and stored on the property and described generally the City’s current hazardous household
waste collection program; benefits of the new facility and services for the public, and project phases
and funding .  Anthony  Rey es, Riske and Salisbury  L aw F irm, repr esenting D avid W. Trefr en
Revocable Trust and Dale D. Trefren Revocable Trust, advised they were opposed to the initial zone
change proposed, but since it has been amended to LI they have no objection at this time.  Mr. Shanor
moved to approve  on second r eading, seconde d by  Mr. Allen.  Conce rn w as raised r egarding
comments received by the various reviewing agencies included as part of the staff report.  Upon
inquiry, Brandon Cammarata, Planning and Development Director, provided information on the zone
change p rocess t hrough t he P lanning C ommission, w hich i ncludes p ublic i nput, a nd f acility
expansion purpose.  He explained the difference between the waste transfer station and household
hazardous waste facility and indicated the reason for the initial CB zone classification many years
ago was unknown; that the gate on Avenue D would provide access to the facility itself and would
not be a public right-of-way access, and that comments by reviewing agencies would be addressed
through the site plan review process.  Upon query, Craig LaVoy, Public Works Department, advised
it is hard to determine how much hazardous household waste is being collected through sanitation
pickups and that expansion of services to the public will be an incentive to residents to not include
these items i n solid waste/regular garbage pickups, whic h may contaminate la ndfills.  Com ment
followed on the ne w facility providing for e xpanded hours of servic e to the public to enc ourage
correct disposal of hazardous items.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body with the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-03-10 | CLIP_ID: 417 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 417

   RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 March 10, 2014
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: MAYOR - Richard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr.
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and
Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Dr. Rinne moved to suspend the rules to change the order of the agenda by moving Agenda
Item #14 (Resolution - Adopting PlanCheye nne) to be acted upon subsequent to Agenda Item #26
(a) (Re-Appointment) and prior to Other Business, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing  body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member  of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by
Dr. Rinne and Mr. Allen, Agenda Item #22(b)  was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Allen
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 24, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Ms. Broyles moved to approve payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to Ross,
Ross and Santini, Dan White, City Attorney, advised the law firm is on retainer by contract to provide
Municipal Court public defender fees, and explained when services are provided.  In conjunction with
a voucher payable to Benchmark Engineers, request was made to provide City Council members with
an update on the Christensen Road  Overpass and Corridor pr oject with May or Kaysen advising
information would be provided by the City Engineer and noting an update on the Public Safety Center
would be provided at a work session scheduled for March 26.  In response to query concerning a
voucher payable to CDW-G Computing Solutions, Lois Huff, City Treasurer, confirmed that payment
was for costs associated with the purchase of multiple items.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote,
Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
 
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, c hanging the  zon ing c lassification f rom CB  Com munity B usiness t o M UR Mixe d-Use

(located at the northwest corner of the intersection of E. 18  Street and Logan Avenue).th   Reporting

Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to LI Light Industrial for Lot
1, Block 1, Public Works Addition, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located on the west side of North College
Drive).  Anthony Reyes, Riske and Salisbury  Law Firm, representing David Trefren and various
trusts he administers, clarified they support the LI zone amendment.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Reyes confirmed at this point
Mr. Trefren does not have  a concern with the propose d LI zone, noting Mr. Tr efren does have a
concern as his residence is in the vicinity, and stated his belief that the gate to be placed on Avenue
D will be locate d upon the weig h station prop erty.  Upon inquiry , Craig LaVoy, Public W orks
Department, stated the road (re: Avenue D) currently is not required to be paved under Phase I of the
project, but under Phase II and III it may be considered, and verified the gate/road portion will not
be for public use.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#4023)

Re-Appointment of Tucker Fagan to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming- Laramie County, State
of Wyoming Joint Powers Board for Frontier Park improvements.  Ms. Broyles moved to approve,
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the
exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointment).
RESOLUTION - Adopting PlanCheyenne: Reflections and Progress, as the Community Plan
for the City  of Chey enne, the  Comprehensive Plan for  portions of L aramie County , and the
Transportation Master Plan for the Cheyenne Urban area.  Prior to calling  for public input on the
item, Mayor Kaysen reviewed public participation speaker guidelines that would apply, including a
time limit of 5 minutes per speaker, due to the number of attendees wishing to provide comments.
The following individuals spoke in support, non-support or in favor of making amendments to the
PlanCheyenne document presented for a doption by the proposed r esolution:  Tim Bielig k; John
Carey; Chris McCall; Carol Matteson Pascal; Glen Chavez; John Volk; Boyd Wiggam, Wyoming
Liberty Group; Frank Cole; Earl Leighan; Lisa Ammons; Joe Dougherty, City Transit program;
Celeste Siemens; Stacy Lynne; Jim Lake; Karen Walden; Laura Jeffrey, South Cheyenne Community
Development Association (SCCDA); Jan Nelson-Schroll; Cheryl Burd; State Representative Mary
Throne; Mike Tuttle; Taylor Haynes; Dick Krahenbuhl; Thomas Hutchings; Tyler Troyer; Lorraine
Grigsby; Laramie County Commissioner M. Lee Hasenauer; State Representative Lynn Hutchings;
Betty Scranton; Albert Simpson; Ken Trowbridge; Judy Meyer; Jeff Moench, and Pete Laybourn.
Topics presented by speakers included the following: Protection of private property owner rights,
liberties and f reedom; U.S. and state constitutional concer ns; community plan as a  guideline for
growth, progress and improvements; infrastructure; public input process; market demand; non-local
consultant firm involved in preparation of the proposed plan; Metropolitan Planning Organization
(MPO) involvement and administrative oversight of the MPO; federal and state funding concerns;
proposed design standards; suggested amendments to the Transportation Master Plan portion of the
document; concern with potential plan effect on disabled, senior and minority populations within the
community, elements of the plan involving complete streets, bike lanes, and amenities such as parks,
trails and open spaces; federal funding support for the transit program; providing choices for private
property owners; state’s recent property issue involving the Environmental Protection A gency;
comparison of proposed City plan to the City of Fort Collins, Colorado, plan; MPO administrative
oversight; opposition to annexation; s upport for r etaining the community’s western heritage and
benefits of tourism; proposed amendments requested by the SCCDA; historic preservation; fair and
appropriate implementation of the plan; philosophy of reasonable growth and planning; concern with
impeding flow of traffic in certain areas, including the downtown area; bicyclist and youth safety;
vision and quality of life issues affiliated with a plan document; free enterprise; suggestion that plan
be voted upon by electors; striking the word “regional” throughout the document; incorporating a
glossary of words a nd phrases within the document; United Nations Agenda  21; sugg estion of
forming a citizens commi ttee; beneficial use of f ederal funds, and c omplexity and leng th of the
PlanCheyenne document.  During  public comments, sever al documents we re read, submitted or
referenced, which are on file with the City Clerk’s office.  Reporting for Committee of the Whole,
Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Brown.  D r. Rinne moved to amend by substitute
resolution dated March 4, 2014, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  It was advised the amendments being
proposed remove some of the restrictive language, such as removing the word “shall” and replacing
it with “should” or “may” as debated at the Committee of the Whole meeting, and addresses issues
involving priva te prope rty rig hts, due defe rence to marke t force s and the priva te sec tor, and
affordable housing and development costs.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” --
Mr. Allen, Mr. B rown, Ms. Broy les, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor , Mr. Valde z and May or
Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Luna and Ms. Williams.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend the substitute to
incorporate Exhi bit A-Amendments date d March  4, 2014, seconde d by  Ms. Broy les.  I t was
confirmed Exhibit A-Amendments was the same document as reviewed and recommended at the
Committee of the Whole meeting.  Ms. Lynne requested clarification on whether there was more than
one Exhibit A.  Dan White, City  Attorney, advised Exhibit  A re ferenced in the resolution is the
PlanCheyenne document and E xhibit A-Amendments is the document involved in the propose d
amendment, which would be an exhibit attached to PlanCheyenne as text amendments.  Ms. Lynne
questioned whether it would be possible to re-name one of the exhibits for clarity of the public on
what is being voted upon.  Discussion included that Exhibit A referenced in the resolution was the
PlanCheyenne document itself and that the document titled Exhibit A-Amendments 3/4/14 involves
17 proposed amendments to the PlanCheyenne (Exhibit A) document.  During discussion, Mr. Allen
raised a point of order inquiring whether a motion had been made or was needed to change the title
of Exhibit A to Exhibit B.  Mayor Kaysen stated a motion could be made.  There being no motion
offered, Mayor Kaysen advised that through discussion, which had included his query to City Council
members and Mr. White concerning any objections to referring to the exhibit as Exhibit B, which
resulted in no objections being raised, that Exhibit A-Amendments would be referred to as Exhibit
B relative to clarification for the public and an understanding by governing body members on what
document was involved in the motion t o amend currently on the floor.  Motion to amend carried.
Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez
and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Luna and Ms. Williams.  Ms. Williams moved to amend the
PlanCheyenne document, Page 17, Plan Amendment Process and Procedures, as follows: (Proposed
Amendment #1) A mendments to PlanChey enne that occur outside of a r egular Comprehensive
Review p rocess a re c onsidered to  be  Pla n A mendments.  T he City  Cou ncil a nd Cou nty
Commissioners may perform Plan A mendments as necessary.  Citizens ma y request P lan
Amendments as ne cessary.  There is no cap to the number of Plan Ame ndments annually.  The
Process is as follows:  1) A directive for an amendment by a governing body or a citizen request for
amendment occurs.  2) Staff reviews the amendment proposal, files a staff report, and forwards the
amendment request to the Planning Commission.  3) The Planning Commission shall hold a public
meeting regarding the amendment request.  4) The amendment request will go before the governing
body that will not have jurisdiction for comments only.  5) The amendment request will go before
the governing body that will have jurisdiction for action.  6) A public meeting will be held and input
will be shared by the public.  The sequence of review steps is to ensure plan consistency.  Approval
of amendments will be by resolution.  Motion died due to lack of a second.  Ms. Williams moved to
amend the PlanCheyenne document and the substitute resolution as previously amended as follows:
(Proposed Amendment #2) Include a glossary of words a nd phrases in the Communi ty Plan as
“Appendix F”.  All other supporting plans (Transportation Master Plan, etc.) will reference Appendix
F.  The glossary will consist of two parts: (1) de finitions, and (2) application impacts.  Part One :
Definitions of words that  are typically used in the planning industry will form the basis of this
glossary.  The litera l meaning of the wor d should be s tated using simple English.  The use  of
additional obscure and confusing vernacular within the definitions of the words will be a voided.
Examples of words to be defined include and are not limited to: livability, walkability, bikeability,
end of ride facility, access management, activity centers, compact design, high density housing,
bicycle box, decision-maker s, overlay  district, active tra nsportation, cultural site, c luster
development, de nsity transfe r, conse rvation over lay, natural/cultural resourc e ar ea, inc entivize,
synthesize public comments, stakeholders, smart g rowth, zero ene rgy development, sustainable ,
public engagement, best practices, models, scientific method, conservation easement, smart meters,
meeting facilitator, vehicular cycling, reframe/rescript/rebrand, and other basic words and phrases
tied to long-range planning documents.  Part Two: Each definition will include a short summary of
the application impacts as related to the concepts and intent of the words and phrases that are being
defined.  (Proposed Amendment #3) Remove planning policies and funding for items that are not
essential government services.  Removal of non-essential government services from PlanCheyenne
satisfies the legal requirements of community planning.  Essential government services are commonly
known as: Fire departments; law enforcement departments; infrastructure systems such as roads,
electricity and water; national security; care of prisoners and others who are in the custody of the
government; emerg ency and disaster  assistance; air traf fic c ontrol; border pa trol ... (Proposed
Amendment #4)  Re move the word “regional” to ensure that decision-making is based on local
control.  Change the word “regional” to “local”.  “Local” will be defined as the entire area that is
legally defined as Laramie County.  (Proposed Amendment #5) Eliminate Policy 5.3.B.  Elimination
of this policy will ensure that private land owner’s rights are protected and the housing market will
be drive n by  actua l market de mand.  Ru ral area s will be protecte d by  preve nting unna tural
“clustering” of homes.  “Clustering” is more consistent with “urban” or “suburban” development.
(Proposed Amendment #6) Eliminate the Future Historic Assets to be Surveyed and Preserved.
Allow individual private owners (both residential and business) to se ek designation for Historic
Preservation for their privately owned property.  This allows property values in Historic Districts to
be market-driven and to remain affordable.  Eliminate the process (though not described) intended
to “ Stabilize pr operty va lues in  Hi storic Districts”.  ( Proposed A mendment # 7) Re move a ll
“incentives” to “encourage infill” so that the real estate industry will be market-driven.  Eliminate
the special exception of “reduced parking requirements” so that the availability of vehicle parking
at residences is protected.  Remove discriminatory practices of expediting development applications
for infill areas.  (Proposed Amendment #8) Describe the procedures and methods that will be used
for “strongly encouraging” a developer to build homes based on the Plan’s requirements.  Add the
descriptions of the procedures and methods into the policy that will be used to strongly encourage
developers.  (Proposed Amendment #9) Eliminate Policy 2.4.A based on interference with private
property rights through use of a Future Land Use Plan that conflicts with the current landowner’s
rights.  Elimination of Policy 2.4.A will ultimately respect the legal protections provided by well-
established contract law and ex post facto laws.  ( Proposed Amendment #10) Remove “Focus on
nightlife” from the Education Policy.  (Proposed Amendment #11) Remove the offering of any and
all “incentives” to residential developers, commercial developers and small business owners.  Allow
the private market to be driven by consumer demand and protect equal competition so that market
demand is naturally fulfilled.  (Proposed Amendment #12) Remove all public funding and programs
for “art in public places” so that the creative industries are market-driven.  Allow private businesses
or individuals to sponsor and/or donate art for public use.  (Proposed Amendment #13) The Cheyenne
Area Master Plan Community Plan, Final Draft February 21, 2014, will be used as the sole reference
document (with the inclusion of all amendments passed after that date).  Specially: if and when the
“streamlined” version as titled above is adopted by the governing body, then the document referred
to as the January 2014 Draft of PlanCheyenne becomes null in its entirety.  The January 2014 Draft
of PlanCheyenne will not be used for reference by any internal city departments, internal county
departments, agencies, boards, commissions, committees, or elected bodies, nor will the January 2014
Draft be referred to for any guidance, policy, or procedures.  (Proposed Amendment #14) All future
actions and implementations required to be considered by the governing bodies as related to policies
and procedures in the Community Plan dated February 21, 2014 and including subsequently adopted
amendments, and the Transportation Plan that is dated February 21, 2014 and including subsequently
adopted amendments, will be prese nted as regul ar agenda items a nd not as items on the consent
agenda.  Each regular agenda item will contain accurate information in order to allow the elected
bodies to responsibly consider the impacts of the specific action to be implemented.  Th at basic
information w ill inc lude a nd is n ot l imited to : 1.  F unding so urce; 2.  Cos ts u sing tr aditionally
established methods of determining total project costs; 3. Confirmed availability of funds prior to
adoption by the governing body; 4. Comparisons of the impact that similar actions have had on other
Front Range Communities.  These comparisons will be made through the use of neutral, third-party
information sources.  Comparisons will not  be made through the use of insider-industry methods
commonly referred to as “best practices”; 5. Scientifically validated evidence that supports the claims
of the project goals, intent, and actions; and 6. Other relevant data that will allow elected officials to
make responsible and informed decisions for their constituents.  (Proposed Amendment #15) All
proposals, additional codes and all policy decisions related to the Transportation Plan will be founded
on actual data.  The use of transportation models to predict future congestion is based on current Land
Use Plans.  Estimations and projections of traffic congestion that will occur in the year 2040 that are
based on c urrent la nd use plans are scie ntifically invalid.  Scientifically  invalid methods and
procedures will not be used to determine transportation planning.  ( Proposed Amendment #16)
Clarify the difference between “Exhibit A” in “A Resolution Adopting PlanCheyenne ...” meaning
the document dated February 21, 2014 referred to as PlanCheyenne: Reflections and Progress and
Exhibit A-Amendments 3/4/14 as presented by Councilor [Dicky] Shanor.  (Proposed Amendment
#17) Include a current Land Use Map in PlayCheyenne: Reflections and Progress that distinctly
illustrates privately owned land that is situated outside of the City of Cheyenne boundary, but that
is inside L aramie County boundarie s.  Proposed Amendment to the “Resolved” sec tion of the
Resolution to include: WHEREAS, Wyo. Stat. Ann. § 9-8-301, § 15-1-503, and § 18-5-202; and 23
U.S. 134 (i)(1)(A) and (i)(B)(ii); and 23 CFR § 450.322 are legal references related to land use plans,
master plans, comprehensive plans, and transportation plans.  AND, Art. 1, § 37 of the Wy oming
Constitution declares that “The State of Wyoming is an inseparable part of the federal union, and the
Constitution of the United States is the supreme law of the land”.  As such, all aforementioned legal
references shall be in compliance w ith the supreme law.  F urther, Art. 1, § 7 of the Wy oming
Constitution states “Absolute arbitrary power over the lives, liberty and property of freemen exists
nowhere in a republic, not even in the largest majority.”  THEREFORE, the guide commonly referred
to as PlanChey enne: Reflections and P rogress shall be inse verable and in complianc e with the
Wyoming Constitution and the Constitution of the United States.  During recitation of the motion by
Ms. Williams, a point of order was raised by Mr. Brown questioning whether all of the proposed
amendments could be incorporated into the record by reference in lieu of them being orally read as
all members of the governing body had previously received a copy of the proposed amendments.
Pursuant to inquiry to Ms. Williams by Mayor Kaysen, Ms. Williams indicated her preference was
to read the amendments orally with Mayor Kaysen concurring.  Pursuant to inquiry to Ms. Williams
by Mayor Kaysen as to whether it was her intention to retain Proposed Amendment #16 in her motion
as i t had been acted upon by  previous motions, Ms. W illiams st ated it was he r intent that the
proposed a mendment (#16) be  included in her  motion.   Motion died due to lack of  a sec ond.
Comment followed that there is an amendment process contained in the current plan which allows
for further amendments to be brought forward by the City Council and County Commissioners and
that the normal City Council meeting and committee process would be followed, which provides the
opportunity for public review and participation.  In response to inquiry, Mayor Kaysen advised even
though this is a joint resolution, the City and County governing bodies have authority to adopt their
own plan and a ny future amendments would involve th e City Council’s usual public proc ess for
awareness and input.  Main motion as twice amended carried.  Before announcing the result of the
votes taken on the motion, Ms. Williams requested, pursuant to Robert’s Rules of Order, that she be
allowed to cha nge her vote from “ yes” to “ no” with May or Kaysen noting the change would be
allowed as the voting results had not yet been stated.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms.
Broyles, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Luna
and Ms. Williams. (#5573)
Under other business, John W alker, attorney, Hickey and Eva ns Law Firm, representing
International A ssociation o f F ire F ighters (I .A.F.F.) L ocal #279, re quested th e City  e ngage in
collective bargaining with his clients.  Mr. Walker stated his understanding that it is the position of
some that the request for collective bargaining was not timely given, noting his disagreement and
indicating that he would not be debating that issue at the meeting.  Mr. Walker represented, based
on research he had c onducted, that the governing body has the powe r and authority to waive a ny
delay in the service  of the re quest and engage in the collec tive bargaining process.  He fur ther
requested if members were unable to fully process the request [tonight] that it be reconsidered at a
later time.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Walker advised it is their position that the deadline was met and the
request was timely submitted.  He expressed his opinion that if the governing body felt the time frame
had not been met, it has the power and authority to waive any delays in the service of the collective
bargaining request.  Mayor Kaysen requested Mr. Walker pr ovide a c opy of his rese arch to Dan
White, City Attorney.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 9:55 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

 March 20, 2014
