MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-03-24 | CLIP_ID: 419 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 419

   RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 March 24, 2014
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.
ABSENT: Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff,
City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing  body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Shanor, and pursuant to support received
by Mr. Allen and Mr. Brown, Agenda Items #8 and #16(b) were removed from the Consent Agenda.
Upon request by Mr. B rown, and pursuant to support re ceived by Mr. Shanor a nd Mr. Allen,
Agenda Items #16(e) (f) and (g) were removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Dr.
Rinne, and pursua nt to support rece ived by Mr. Shanor a nd Mr. Allen, Age nda Item #9 was
removed from the Consent Ag enda.  Mr . Allen  moved to appro ve remaining Consent Agenda
items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 10, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to AP
Wyoming relative to services provided for the municipal pool renovation and expansion project,
Rick P arish, Parks & Rec reation Director, pr ovided an e xplanation of pre -construction fees
affiliated with project services by the vendor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown
returned to Council Chambers.

Finance Committ ee, Mr . Br own moved to appr ove in an a mount not to ex ceed $76,720.00,

advised the amendment will provide clarification and should be workable for the provider under
the scope of work.  H e noted if the  contractor should have a concern with the amendment, a ny
changes would come back to the g overning body as a c ontract modification.  Motion to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Upon inquiry, Jim Flesher,
Planning Department, clarified there was no services delivery date in the agreement, noting it is
anticipated the  study coul d be complete d next fall or winter followed by  an ordina nce to be
presented to the governing body.  Comment followed that the study topic was not time sensitive in
nature; concern was expressed with an open- ended date to rec eive the study .  Mai n motion as
amended carried.  Voting “ yes” - all members of  the g overning body present.  May or Kaysen
suggested quarterly reports on the status of the study be given to Council members due to the open-
ended date. (#6141)

noted  in paragraph 7, which will be corrected.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body present. (#6146)

[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-19-14 for furnishing 76 new desktop computers for the City of
Cheyenne, Information Technology Division, pursuant to the City’s replacement program (2011 -
2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept bid from CDW Government
LLC, Vernon Hills, IL; amount not to exceed $49,260.16).

Department, a nd Re iman Corporation &  Sub sidiary of  Che yenne, Wy oming, f or the  Wes t
Lincolnway Streetscape project (State & Local Grants, Miscellaneous Federal Grants) -- referred

containers for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division (Impact Assistance Fund) -- referred to

vehicles for the City of Cheyenne, Parking Division (Impact Assistance Fund) -- referred to Finance
Committee. 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 14, 2014 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2 101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a tra nsfer of loca tion for a
microbrewery permit application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Freedoms Edge Brewing Co.,
LLC d/b/a Freedoms Edge Brewing Co., 1509 Pioneer Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming (license
currently held by Freedom’s Edge Brewing Co., LLC d/b/a Freedoms Edge Brewing Co., 301 W.
16  Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming).th
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, April 14, 2014 at 6:00
p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for Fiscal Year 2014-
2015 Annual Ac tion Pl an, City of Chey enne Community Development Block G rant (CDBG)
entitlement.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 14, 2014 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a bar and grill liquor license
application filed with the City Clerk's Office, for WRCH-Cheyenne, Inc. d/b/a Wyoming’s Rib and
Chop House, 400 W. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
Announcement was made of the Re-Appointments of John M. Jolley and David A. Pulley,
to the International Fire Code Board of Appeals, to be confirmed April 14, 2014. 
Under other business, Da vid Evans, attorne y, Hickey and Eva ns law fir m, representing
International Association of Fire Fighters (I.A.F.F.) Local #279, requested a motion be made and
passed by the governing body at the current meeting to waive any missed deadline relative to the
Union’s request to enter into collective bargaining, noting the Union’s position is that the deadline
was met and the request was timely submitted.  Mr. Evans commented in support of his request and
presented concerns and potential issues if a new contract is not in place; advised a grievance had
been filed on this date; referenced a previous opinion that had been submitted at the last City
Council meeting regarding authority to waive the deadline, and responded to inquiries posed by City
Council members.  Comments and inquiries prese nted by Council members included: Whether
collective bargaining discussions could begin sooner to coincide with the annual budget preparation;
timing of past requests relative to s tate statute provisions and annual state legislative sessions;
quorum of the governing body required to conduct collective bargaining meetings, and the number
of times collective bargaining has resulted in arbitration.
There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning body, the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 6:47 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

 April 7, 2014

   RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 April 14, 2014
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  P resent were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna and Mr. Valdez.  ABSENT:
Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff,
City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  Mayor Kaysen recognized members of the Mighty
Eagles 4-H Club present at the meeting and requested they lead the recitation of the pledge of
allegiance.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing  body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Allen moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 24, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Council inquiry was made concerning vouchers
payable to the following:  BT Real Estate (property value assessment), Gary’s Paint and Restoration
Performance (repair work on 2 vehicles), Clerk of District Court (payment for evidence received
affiliated with a settled court case), Elwood Staffing (temporary labor for West Side Story Civic
Center production), Bob Bradshaw (reimbursement, as the Division Head, to the Parking Division’s
petty cash fund for lot refunds), and Holland & Hart (outside legal services to represent the City in
a Wyoming State Bar complaint).  Providing information available in response to the inquiry were
Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director; Vicki Nemecek, Assistant Director, Public Works; Lois
Huff, City Treasurer, and Mayor Kaysen.  Motion car ried.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to r eceive comments and input for  a tra nsfer of
location for a microbrewery permit application filed with the City  Clerk's Office for Freedom’s
Edge Brewing Co., LLC d/b/a Freedom’s Edge Brewing Co., 1509 Pioneer  Avenue, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (license currently held by Freedom’s Edge Brewing Co., LLC d/b/a Freedom’s Edge
Brewing Co., 301 W. 16  Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming).  There being no comments or input andth
purpose of  the hea ring having  been me t, May or Ka ysen closed the  hear ing and referred the
application to Finance Committee.
 Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing for Fiscal Year 2014-2015 Annual Action Plan, City
of Chey enne Community  Deve lopment Block Gr ant (CDB G) e ntitlement.  Deanne  Widauf,
Housing and Community Development Office, provided a brief overview of the history and purpose
of the CDBG program, noting the City  has re ceived entitlement g rant funds since  1975.  She
advised t he proposed plan is consistent with the 5 y ear Comprehensive Consolidated Plan
completed in 2010, and stated the anticipated allocation funding amount is $406,045.00, which is
8% less than last year.  Ms Widauf explained the grant application process, public notifications and
opportunities for input, and role of the Advisory Council in reviewing and recommending projects
to be funde d.  She stated six projects are pr oposed under the ca tegories of public se rvices and
general services, and noted funding for program administration.  She reviewed the projects and
funding amounts as recommended by the Advisory Council.  There being no further comments and
input, and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a bar and grill
liquor l icense a pplication f iled w ith the  City Cle rk's O ffice, f or W RCH-Cheyenne, Inc. d /b/a
Wyoming’s R ib a nd C hop House, 400 W . Lincolnway, C heyenne, W yoming.  U pon i nquiry,
Preston Chiasson, applicant, clarified the application is for a new bar and grill liquor license, not
a transfer of an e xisting license.  There being no further comments or input and purpose  of the
hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the application to Finance
Committee.

Consideration of Bid #M-14-14 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering

(2011 - 2014 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.

Construction Division, and STC Construction Co., Inc. for the Yellowstone Road Wear Course

for the Information Technology Division to provide appropriate network bandwidth for the new

Re-Appointments of John M. Jolley and David A. Pulley, to the International Fire Code
Board of Appeals.  Ms. Broy les moved to approve , seconded by Mr. B rown.  Motion car ried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Kaysen
who did not vote (Mayoral appointments).
Under other business, the following individuals expressed concerns and dissatisfaction with
the upco ming change in Sanitation’s automated solid waste c ollection services for re sidential
containers which involves changing the pick-up location from alleys to street curbside collection
points in certain areas within the City: Major Dorr, Kevin Robinett, Mary Liz Langley, Jeannine
Melka, Jan Nelson-Schroll, Jane Robinett, Larry Melka, Larry and Peggy Rosentreter, Betty Sue
Allen, Pete Laybourn and Kevin Vance.  Topics presented included: Sanitation’s study and analysis
of pick-up routes; beautification; curbside collections posing a hardship for senior citizens and
handicapped individuals; collection efficiencies; fairness and objectivity in determining collection
points; property damage occurring due to garbage trucks negotiating alleys, possible solutions to
retain alley pick-ups; previous resolution on the topic that failed adoption in 2012; concerns with
container storage site availability in certain neighborhoods and timely retrieval of containers from
streets; image and property values; solid waste fee study and potential enterprise budget fund for
Sanitation and cost of Sanitation’s new GPS system.  Duri ng comments and pursuant to public
inquiry regarding the methodology in not presenting the operational change in the for m of a
resolution, Dan White, City Attorney, advised that due to potential attorney-client conflict he would
decline to publicly discuss the matter at this time.  Information on the purpose of collection point
changes and response to comments/inquiries posed by speakers and members of the governing body
were provided by Vicki Nemecek, Assistant Director of Public Works, and Dennis Pino, Director
of Sanitation.  Discussion of the topic by members of the governing body followed and included
inquiries affiliated with concerns that had been raised; postcards advising of the upcoming change
that had been sent by the City; route efficiencies; Sanitation drivers’ input on safety concerns and
problems with maneuvering Sanitation trucks in alleys; Sanitation staff collection assistance and
efforts; GPS programming; impact fee study; alley encroachments; driver training, and operational
issues.  During discussion a letter from Jim Rose, requesting reconsideration of discontinuing alley
collections, was read into the record by Mr. Allen.  Mayor Kaysen expressed appreciation for the
public’s comments and advised the topic would be reviewed.  Ms. Nelson-Schroll commented that
a valuable piece of information would be a one-page financial synopsis of Sanitation income and
expenses for the past year, and requested she be provided with the information. 
There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning body, the meeting  was
officially adjourned  at 8:28 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 April 24, 2014

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-04-28 | CLIP_ID: 421 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 421

   RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 April 28, 2014
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  P resent were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor and
Mr. Valdez.  ABSENT: Ms. Williams.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing  body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Shanor moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 14, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
ORDINANCE - Amending the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC), to provide
for a n Alterna tive Compli ance for r educed residential minimum fron t setback.  B randon
Cammarata, Director of Planning and Development, provided information on setback distances for
single family homes and duplexes, from 25 ft. to 15 ft. when certain criteria is met and particularly
for infill developments in existing neighborhoods, pursuant to inquiry that had been raised at the
Public Services Committee meeting.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor moved
to approve on second reading, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Cammarata clarified
garages will still need to meet setback criteria.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the
governing body present.
RESOLUTION - Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2014 budget of the City of Cheyenne
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113.  Lois Huff, City Treasurer, requested
consideration of a two week postponement to allow time to evaluate other potential funding sources
(relative to funding needed for firefighter wages as the result of arbitration last year) pursuant to
discussion held at the Finance Committee meeting.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Allen
advised there was no recommendation on the item from the Committee.  Mr. Allen moved to adopt,
seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Mr. Allen moved to postpone to May 12, 2014, seconded by Dr. Rinne.
Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.

RESOLUTION - Rescinding Resolution No. 3451 and establishing a new schedule of fees,
charges and expenses as required by Section 1.3.7. of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code.
Matt Ashby , Direc tor of Planning  Service s, provided a br ief ove rview of  the purpose of  the
resolution, noting the last fee increases were 21 years ago.  He discussed administrative actions and
processes involving fees; cost of services; fee structures and amounts studied involving other
communities; “hard” costs incurre d, and L aramie County’s ra te structure .  Dale Stee nbergen,
President, G reater Ch eyenne Ch amber o f Co mmerce, s poke in  o pposition to  th e r esolution,
commenting on assumptions that were being made pertaining to community development costs;
previous Cheyenne LEADS study; relationship of fees affecting local businesses and economic

Emmons, AVI  Eng ineering, posed questions reg arding simple and complex development
agreements and the diff erence be tween site pla ns; fi nancial security amount, and subdivision
standard waiver, and suggested increases be put in place over a 3-4 year period.  Mr. Allen moved
to adopt, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Discussion included the following topics: Notes/clarifications
that were not included with the r esolution information; percentage of c ost currently bourne by
developers vs. administrative costs expended; deter mining costs to do business in Chey enne;
pending results of the development impact study; competitiveness, and proposed flat fees.  During
discussion and upon inquiry, Mr. Ashby provided information on the flat fees proposed; County fee
structure, which is based on a flat rate as well as an hourly staff time rate; “hard” costs involving
such things as advertising of legal notifications; mailings, re ports, etc., and recent development
construction valuations.  He stated the impac t study will address how to ha ndle growth issues
involving City services.  Mr. Brown moved to postpone to May 12, 2014, seconded by Ms. Broyles.
Following comment o n r eceiving fu rther i nformation o n th e “ package” of  fe es a nd c osts t o

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-05-12 | CLIP_ID: 423 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 423

   RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 May 12, 2014
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  P resent were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna and Dr. Rinne.  ABSENT:
Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff,
City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing  body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by
Ms. Broyles and Mr. Allen, Agenda Item #13 was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Allen
moved to approve  remaining Consent Ag enda items, sec onded by Mr. C ook.  Motion carr ied.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 28, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
ORDINANCE - Amending the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC), to provide
for an Alternative Compliance for reduced residential minimum front setback.  B oyd Wiggam,
Wyoming Liberty Group, expressed concerns with the proposed ordinance relative to wording in
Section 4; questioned whethe r new in-fill developments would be a llowed to match wha t was
similar in the area or held to standards for the majority of houses previously built in a neighborhood,
and suggested an amendment to Section 4 for clarification be considered.  Donald James Howard
Perrott Macneil commented in opposition to development in general.  Reporting for Public Services
Committee, Mr. Allen move d to approve on third and fina l rea ding, se conded by Mr. Cook.
Comment followed on the proposed or dinance, as written, providing flexibility and meeting the
desired purpose of the amendment to the UDC.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body present. (#4024)
ORDINANCE - Approving and establishing revised water and sewer rates, tap and system
development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties and septic and commercial
waste dump rates, as recommende d by  the B oard of  Public Utili ties -- re ferred to F inance
Committee (sponsor - Mr. Brown).
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne U nified
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to MUB
Mixed-Use Business Emphasis for L ot 1, Block 1, Ta k Subdivis ion and L ot 1, Block 1, D an
Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located north of and a djacent to  W. Lincolnway, between
Westland Road and I-25) -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor - Mr. Shanor).  
ORDINANCE - Amending Sections 2.76.020(A) and (B), Members-Terms-Vacancies, of
Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the
code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor - Dr.
Rinne).
ORDINANCE - Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming for the various
purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing the amount of general and
special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said appropriation all for the Fiscal Year
beginning July 1, 2014 and ending June 30, 2015 -- referred to Committee of the Whole (sponsor -
Mr. Brown).
ORDINANCE - Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste fee schedule established by
Ordinance No. 4013 -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor - Mr. Brown).
RESOLUTION - Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2014 budget of the City of Cheyenne
according to the provisions of W.S . §16-4-112 a nd 16-4-113 (postponed from 04/28/14). 
Reporting for F inance Committee, Mr. B rown moved to a dopt, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mr.
Brown moved to amend by substitute dated May 2, 2014, seconded by Ms. Broyles. It was advised
the amendment changes the funding source for some line items from impact assistance funds to
unanticipated additional general revenue, and tha t the total dollar amount remained the same . 
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Main motion
as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#5585)
RESOLUTION - Rescinding Resolution No. 3451 and establishing a new schedule of fees,
charges and expenses as required by Section 1.3.7. of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code.
Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, referenced a staff memo dated May 5, 2014 that had been
submitted to governing body members and provided an overview of the information contained in
the memo affiliated with clarifications; proposed preliminary and final plat costs; utilizing a flat rate
vs. a flat rate plus a per lot cost for platting activities, and implementing fee increases on a “phased
in” approach.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown stated there was no recommendation
on the item from Committee.  Mr. B rown moved to a dopt, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Discussion
topics included: Previously approved action to acquire a Development Impact Fee Study prepared
by a consultant and anticipated time frame for receipt of the information; Chamber of Commerce’s
proposal to conduct and pay for a study relative to the proposed resolution topics and anticipated
time frame for receipt; participation by City staff during the Chamber’s process of compiling its
study; concern that the Chamber’s estimated 90 day time frame in which to submit its study results
may not be sufficient to cover the comprehensive areas to be involved; various subjects that should
be objec tively considere d i n the Chamber’s study  (such a s acquisition of wate r rig hts and
infrastructure) involving costs of doing business within the Cit y; justifiable fee increases and/or
decreases, and governing body’s authority pertaining to recommendations that may be presented
based upon the  Chamber’ s study.  During  discussion and upo n inquiry, Mr. Ashby  provided
information concerning the Development Impact Fee Study being conducted by the consultant, and
Dan White, City Attorney, advised as a general proposition the governing body is not bound by
recommendations of any study that is presented to them.  Dale Steenbergen, Greater Cheyenne
Chamber of Commerce, provided information in response to inquiries and advised the Chamber’s
study approach would be a comprehensive review of fees affiliated with the cost to develop within
the community.  He stated a comprehensive study may take longer than 90 days, that a joint ad hoc
task force could serve as the study work group, and that updates to the governing body during the
process would be through reports submitted twice a month.  He confirmed designated City staff
input would be involved during the study process as well as accepting input from the community,
and that the goal was that the business community would not be left out of the disc ussions.  Mr.
Steenbergen commented on e conomic deve lopment, costs of deve lopment, and pay ing f or
infrastructure on a  long- term basis.  May or Ka ysen provided inf ormation on m eetings of the
governing body during the month of August pursuant to the Chamber’s estimated 90 day time frame
in which to submit t he study or a summary status report, and possibility of a City Council work
session.  Mr. Steenbergen commented that perhaps a meeting with City Council members should
be held soon to understand the structure and topics to be included in the study .  Motion to adopt
failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body present.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-05-27 | CLIP_ID: 426 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 426

   RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 May 27, 2014
 
Due to the Memorial Day holiday, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne instead met
in regular session on this date beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR
- Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr.
Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez.  ABSENT: Ms. Broyles  and Ms. Williams.  Also present:  Carol
Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of
allegiance, led by members of the Mayor’s Youth Council, was recited.
Expression of Appreciation to Mayor’s Youth Council and State of the Youth Report.
Juliette Givhan, Chair of the Mayor’s Youth Council, provided a brief presentation of the group’s
activities and learning experiences during the past year.  She noted the members’ involvement with
local service groups and community issues affecting youth; leadership and educational opportunities
provided through discussions with local government officials and staff, and tours of facilities and
operations.  Jan Spires, Mayor’s Youth Council Coordinator, commended members of the 2013-
2014 Youth Council on their commitment and involvement.  With assistance from Katrina Peterson,
who also provided administrative support for this year’s Youth Council, Mayor Kaysen presented
Certificates of Appreciation to the following Youth  Council members:  Jul iette Givhan (Chair),
Alex Cole (Vice-Chair), Caroline Alston, Elizabeth Babbitt, Tyler Bedwell, Alex Farkas, Alexis
Ferrari, Rachel Moseley, Emily Piel, Kesslee Stewart, Jason Weber and Darnell Wheeler Jr.  Mayor
Kaysen expressed appreciation to members for their time, commitment and dedication, and also to
members’ families for their support.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing  body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Shanor  moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on May  12, 20 14.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to Council
Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive public comments regarding the intent of
the City of Cheyenne to submit an application to the Wyoming Business Council’s Business Ready
Community, Community Readiness Grant Program, for the Christensen Railroad O verpass and
Corridor Project.  Tom Mason, Director, Metropolitan Planning Organization, reviewed the intent
of the grant program and provided information on the project.  He advised the grant amount to be
requested is $3,000,000, which would involve a local match of $1,500,000; noted an application
for a competitive federal TIGER grant has also been submitted; stated the total estimated cost of
the project is $13.4 million dollars, and provided information on other potential funding sources,
including leveraging of grant funds if awarded.  J. Fred Volk spoke in support of the application
request and transportation connectivity benefit to the neighborhoods in the area.  Upon inquiry, Mr.
Mason stated public safety benefits could be realized due to the existing City Fire Training Center
in the vicinity and it’s capability of being utilized as a fire station in the future.  The re being no
further input and comments and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen
closed the hearing.  Mayor Kaysen noted Agenda Item #25 (Resolution) was related to the public
hearing topic and would be reviewed at the Finance Committee meeting on June 2, 2014.
ORDINANCE - Approving and establishing revised water and sewer rates, tap and system
development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties and septic and commercial
waste dump ra tes, as recommended by the B oard of  Public Utili ties.  Reporting  for F inance
Committee, Mr. Brown moved to a pprove on second reading, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Offic ial Z oning Ma p, of the Chey enne U nified
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to MUB
Mixed-Use Business Emphasis for Lot 1, Block 1, Tak Subdivision and Lot 1, Block 1, Dan
Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located north of and a djacent to W . Lincolnway, between
Westland Road and I -25).  B oyd W iggam, Wyoming Liberty Group, spoke in support of  the
proposed ordinance to allow private landowners and developers to meet community needs, noting
his belief the action meets the general goals of PlanCheyenne and would provide for affordable
housing (apartments).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Allen stated there was no
recommendation on the item from Committee.  M r. Allen advised it had been brought to his
attention that following the Committee meeting, the agent for the applicant requested the item be
withdrawn from the ag enda.  F ollowing query to members of the  governing body and with no
objection being raised, Mayor Kaysen announced the item was being withdrawn from the agenda.
ORDINANCE - Amending Sections 2.76.020(A) and (B), Members-Terms-Vacancies, of
Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the
code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Allen moved to approve on second reading, seconded
by Dr. Rinne.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
ORDINANCE - Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming for the various
purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing the amount of general and
special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said appropriation all for the Fiscal Year
beginning July 1, 2014 and ending  June 30, 2015.  Reporting  for Committee of the Whole, Dr.
Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body present.  A summary of the proposed budget is as follows:
General Fund, $47,229,809; Weed and Pest, $510,000; Youth Alternatives Gr ants, $772,190;
Development Impact Fees, $151,000; Recreation Programs, $1,049,027; Community Development
Block Grant, $408,118; 1% Sales Tax Fund, $10,160,180; Law Enforcement Grants, $164,880;
Transportation Planning, $828.209; FTA Transit Grant, $2,554,151; Juvenile Justice, $235,536;
Special Friends, $145,667; Special Purpose Option Tax, $172,484; Youth Activities, $66,410; Golf
Facilities, $221,000; Fleet Maintenance, $5,195,966; Solid Waste Management Fund, $14,729,637;
Civic Center, $973,300; Ice and Events Center, $542,718; Ceme tery Perpetual Fund, $20,000 -
Total City proposed budget - $86,130,282.  Water Department, $35,830,775; Sewer Department,
$24,239,525 - Total Board of Public Utilities proposed budget - $60,070,300, for a total proposed
budget appropriation of $146,200,582.
 ORDINANCE - Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste fee schedule established by
Ordinance No. 4013.  Mr. Brown moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, verified a draft of the rate study is in the process
of being reviewed by staff and that a work session with the governing body will be scheduled to
discuss the rep ort and recommendations from the c onsultant.  Discussion followed on budg et
structure c hanges in volving so lid wa ste fu nds f or F Y2015; implementation a nd tim e fr ame
regarding the rate study; rates being tied to the c ost of operations and not nece ssarily a Cost of
Living Adjustment (COL A) or c onsumer price index; incremental incr eases; planning ( cost of
development) study which is in progress vs. regular daily sanitation business costs, and timing of
the fee schedule increase per the proposed ordinance vs. approval of the final rate study.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting
“no” -- Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Mr. Valdez.
ORDINANCE - Amending various sections of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code
(UDC), to allow Service Users, Animal Care - Hospital/Clinic in the CBD Central Business District
after conditional discretionary review -- r eferred to Publi c Services Committee (sponsor -- Mr .
Shanor).

[CA] Contract between the Wyoming Department of Family Services and the City of Cheyenne,

Professional Servic es Ag reement betwee n the City of Chey enne on be half of the
Metropolitan Planning O rganization and Cam bridge Sy stematics, I nc., for  the Chey enne
Transportation Sa fety Ma nagement P lan u pdate (N ational H ighway Tr ansportation Sa fety
Administration, Highway Safety Grant through Wyoming Department of T ransportation).  Mr.
Brown reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown
moved to approve, seconded  by Mr. Allen.  Sreyoshi Chakraborty, MPO, advised the review and
update to the Plan involves compilation of transportation accident and crash data to identify areas
of concern involving fatalities and injuries and to provide long and short term strategies affiliated
with safety issues.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Chakraborty advised the information will be shared with
other agencies, such as the Safety Advisory Committee, Police Department, Wyoming Department
of Transportation and schools, and clarified federal funding has been received for the project.  Upon
further inquiry, she explained corridor design plans conducted through the MPO looks at different
elements, such as engineering, to address transportation safety improvements whereas the emphasis
of the transportation plan is to address problematic areas and behavioral issues, such as distracted
driving.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#6173)

for full time city employees.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6174)

assist in the construction of a rail spur, switching, and related rail facilities to serve the Swan Ranch
Rail Park, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and including financing statements and collateral assignments
necessary to close the loan transaction.  Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr.
Brown moved to amend by substitute dated 5/16/14, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Upon inquiry, Dan
White, City Attorney, provided information on changes contained within the substitute involving
the inclusion of signatures lines for the Wyoming Business Council, minor editorial changes in
Schedule 2, and a Consent to Lien.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present.  Main motion as amended c arried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present. (#6175)

Year 2014 City Overlay Project (2011- 2014 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.
Appointment of Barry Sims to the Visit Cheyenne Board.  Mr. Brown moved to approve,
seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present
with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointment).
Re-Appointments of Amiee Reese and Scott French to the Visit Cheyenne Board, and Larry
Atwell to the Cheyenne/Laramie County Economic Development Joint Powers Board.  Dr. Rinne
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body  prese nt with the exception of May or Ka ysen who did not vote (Ma yoral
appointments).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held June 9, 2014 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether compliance
of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land situated in a
portion of the Northeast Quarter of the Northwest Quarter of Section 26, Township 14 North, Range
66 West of the 6th P.M., L aramie County, Wyoming, to be known as the Del-Van Storage Park
Annexation (located at 5311 Dell Range Boulevard at the southwest corner of the intersection of
Gysel Place and Dell Range Boulevard).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held June 9, 2014 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for proposed budgets for the City
of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2015. 
[CA] Request to approve an additional dispensing room for City of Cheyenne Limited Retail
Liquor Licensee AMVETS Pos t 10, located a t 3839 East L incolnway, Cheyenne Wyoming --
referred to Finance Committee.
Under other business:
Joe Dougherty, Director, City of Cheyenne Transit Program, encouraged the governing body
to find a wa y to start the process to implement the Employee Investment Study, commenting on
current salary levels and related market competitiveness that affect work force staffing levels.  It
was noted the City is waiting for a court decision regarding a law suit filed by the local newspaper
affiliated with the EIS before moving forward with results of the study.
Mr. Allen commented on the difficulty sanitation workers are experiencing in getting trucks
through many alleys because of interfering tree branches, and encouraged citizens to help by
trimming their trees in the alley ways.
There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning body, the meeting  was
officially adjourned  at 7:20  p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 June 5, 2014

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-06-09 | CLIP_ID: 428 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 428

    RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 June 9, 2014
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  P resent were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - M r. B rown, Mr . Co ok, M r. L una, D r. Rin ne, M r. Sha nor a nd Ms.  Willi ams.
ABSENT: Mr. Allen, Ms. Broyles and Mr. Valdez.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois
Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing  body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Cook , and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Brown and Mr. Shanor, Ag enda Item #25(j) was removed fr om the Consent Ag enda.  Mr .
Brown moved to appr ove remaining Consent Ag enda items, sec onded by Mr. L una.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Governing Body on May  27, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown
returned to Council Chambers.
 Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to determine w hether compliance of annexation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land situated in a portion of the
Northeast Quarter of the Northwest Quarter of Section 26, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of
the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be known as the Del-Van Storage Park Annexation
(located at 5311 Dell Range Boulevard at the southwest corner of the intersection of Gysel Place
and Dell Range Boulevard).  Jim Flesher, Planning Services Department, reviewed requirements
and compliance of conditions contained in Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402, including specific information
indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i) through (vi).  Casey Palma, Steil Surveying
Services, agent for the applicant, stated the proposed pr operty meets the required conditions for
annexation and the parcel is tied to a lar ger development project area.  He advised a request for
postponement may be made at the upcoming Public Services Committee meeting as they are in the
process of scheduling neighborhood meetings to discuss the development project.  Upon inquiry,
it was advised the pr operty owner requested annexation of the parc el.  Ther e being no further
comments or input and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the
hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing for the purpose of receiving input and comments on
the proposed budgets for the City of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2015.
Lois Huff, City Treasurer, noted the budget has been presented and discussed at a Council work
session as well as prior Council and Committee of the Whole meetings.  She stated the general fund
budget proposed is in the amount of $47,229,809.00 with a total proposed budget amount of
$146,200,582.00, which includes the Board of Public Utilities (BOPU) water and sewer budgets.
Ms. Huff clarified the budget includes provisions for a 2.25% salary increase for non-uniformed and
sworn police officers, an additional contribution of .75% to the Wyoming retirement system for
eligible employees and a 6.25% increase for health insurance coverage.  She advised two additional
changes in the budget this year are the Solid Waste fund and allocation of charges to departments
for Fleet Maintenance services.  Ed Strader questioned the “indexing” and rate classes affiliated
with BOPU’s fee structure and commented on BOPU’s expenses (bonding) and fixed indebtedness.
There being no further comments or discussion and purpose of the public hearing having been met,
Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
ORDINANCE - Approving and establishing revised water and sewer rates, tap and system
development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties and septic and commercial
waste dump ra tes, as re commended by  the B oard of  Publi c Utilities.  Ed Strade r expressed
opposition to how fees are assessed for multi-family residential units vs. single family residential
dwellings, ref erencing a ha nd-out he provide d during  his comments, and spoke in fa vor of
equalizing the r ates.  Mr. Strade r also stated his conc erns with “inde xing” water ra tes to the
Consumer P rice Index.  L ea Bain r equested c larification r egarding the  rates f or sin gle family
dwellings vs. multi-family units.  Tim Wilson, BOPU Director, provided information on the rate
structure, advising the structure treats multi-family and single family dwellings equitably, explained
the rate design and reviewed fee examples based on the proposed rate.  Reporting for Finance
Committee, Mr. Brown advised there was no recommendation on the item from Committee.  Mr.
Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Mr. Shanor moved
to amend by  omitti ng the proposed inc rease amount to tap and sy stem development fe es as
evidenced in Exhibit C affiliated with the ordinance, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Discussion followed
on receipt of informa tion from the deve lopment fee study prior to approving the fe e increase,
proposed 3.4% increase based on the index, and intent of the proposed amendment, which is to keep
tap and system development fees at the current rate.  U pon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney,
stated his understanding of the motion to amend, which is to eliminate the proposed increase to tap
and system development fees only with no effect on the current rate.  Upon inquiry regarding how
much the increase in fees would generate, Mr. Wilson advised the tap and system development fees
increase as proposed in Exhibit C would be effective Jan. 1, 2015, if approved, and would generate
approximately $36,500.00 for water and $16, 300.00 for sewer funds.  Motion to amend  carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Discussion followed on a meeting held
at the library last year and information that was provided concerning BOPU’s fee structure.  Randy
Hays, BOPU, advised he would provide information from the meeting to members of the governing
body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present
with the exception of Mr. Luna voting “no”. (#4025)
ORDINANCE - Amending Sections 2.76.020(A) and (B), Members-Terms-Vacancies, of
Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the
code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor
moved to approve on third and f inal reading, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting

Contract between the City of Cheyenne and WINhealth Partners for employee Wellness
Coaching Services for the term of July 1, 2014 through June 30, 2015.  Rich Wiederspahn, Human
Resources Director, provided information on the effects of the program since its implementation;
tracking of data for the different risk levels (high, medium and low), and spoke in support of
continuing the Wellness P rogram for participating employees.  He  reviewed the City’s health
insurance coverage plans available as opti ons to eligible employees and difference in r ates to
employees in the pr ogram vs. those who c hoose not to be in the prog ram, and elements of the
program that participants must meet, such as a n annual health risk assessm ent, blood draw and
wellness coaching sessions.  He advised WINhealth is open to suggestions and ideas on how to
improve the program.  Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Upon inquiry, Mr.
Wiederspahn provided information on how employees have the choice to opt out of the program
and the effect of same on the premium amount that would be assessed to the employee (i.e. 25%
vs. 12.5% contribution rate), noting the Wellness Program is an incentive to lower the premium
amount paid by a participating employee.  Discussion included financial hardship to employees and
their families if they opt out of the program and the appearance of penalizing the employee; that no
incentives are offered to those employees who reduce their risk level; effect of the program based
on percentage of employee participation; cost of the program; potential for increased premiums if
the program was discontinued; personal lifestyle choices, and suggestion that the program elements
and benefits should be further  reviewed.  Upon inquiry, Lois Huff, City Treasurer, advised the
higher premium amounts paid by employees who are not in the program go into the general fund
to help pay costs.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” -- Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr.
Brown, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.

Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held June 23, 2014 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Ch ambers, 2 101 O ’Neil A venue, Ch eyenne, Wy oming, to  re ceive public c omments
regarding the intent of the City of Cheyenne to designate the use of excess monies from 2011-2014
1% Optional Sales Tax funds for r ight-of-way maintenance, for the L ake Minnehaha Phase I
Project, and for prioritized energy efficiency projects in City-owned facilities.
Under other business, Pete Laybourn submitteded a handout, titled Deploy Bucket Brigades
for Cheyenne Frontier Days, an element contained in a past Placemaking report, to governing body
members a nd encour aged implementation of its rec ommendations re garding c leaning and
beautification of downtown a reas prior to the upcoming Cheyenne Frontier Days event.  Mr.
Laybourn’s comments included the following topics: Downtown alley grease spill, Clean and Safe
program, banners and trees.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen advised the Downtown Development
Association (DDA) is planning to replace some trees in the downtown area; that the Placemaking
plan was a dopted as a   g uideline, containing  a number  of re commendations, and that
implementation is based on funding and resources.  Comment was made concerning the condition
of a pillar and sidewalk near the DDA’s downtown office. 

There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning body, the meeting  was
officially adjourned  at 7:42 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

 June 24, 2014

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-06-23 | CLIP_ID: 431 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 431

    RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 June 23, 2014
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and
Ms. Williams.  ABSENT: Mr. Cook.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received. Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Shanor moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
Presentation of Wyoming Association of Municipalities (WAM) 2014 Innovations Awards
and Community Hero Awards.  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, reviewed awards the City of Cheyenne
recently received at the annual WAM convention as follows: Innovation Awards (projects/programs)
-- Development Standards Help Accommodate New Development, Mayor’s Youth Council Bullying
Project, Sanitation Routing Software, Roundabout Education, and Cone Zone Map; Community
Hero Awards (community enhancement projects) -- Cheyenne Police Department for its Citizens
Police Academy and Citizens on Patrol programs, Cheyenne Greenway Foundation, and the North
Cheyenne Community Park Partners for the soft surface trail system (Community Partners involved
with funding and materials assitance were: Paul Frauendienst, owner, Frauendienst Quality Homes;
Dan Gregg, owner, Gregg Construction; Larry Sutherland, real estate agent, #1 Properties, and Frank
Cole, local developer.  Mayor Kaysen recognized City staff and members of the community present
at the meeting who were involved in the projects and programs.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Mee ting of the Gover ning Body on J une 9, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Radio
Station KYOY-AM) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Valdez
returned to Council Chambers.
 Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive public comments regarding the intent of
the City of Cheyenne to designate the use of excess monies from 2011-2014 1% Optional Sales Tax
funds for right-of-way maintenance, for the Lake Minnehaha Phase I Project, and f or prioritized
energy efficiency projects i n City-owned facilities.  Vi cki Nemecek, Assistant Director, Public
Works, noted the heari ng was being he ld in conjunction with an aff iliated ag enda item ( i.e.
Resolution) and explained 1% Optional Sales Tax provisions regarding use of unanticipated overage
money.  She advised the estimated overage amount was $863,119.00 and reviewed the proposed uses
for the funds, noting the projects are listed in priority order.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek and Todd
Feezer, Assistant Director, Parks and Recreation Department, provided information in response to
queries concerning right-of-way maintenance areas involved, equipment, staffing  and budg eted
funding; Lake Minnehaha Phase I and proposed Phase II, when funding is available, and facilities
energy efficiency improvements to several city-owned buildings.  It was noted that the governing
body had prioritized 1% projects; staff had reviewed and prioritized the proposed projects for use
of the overage funds, and that the administration is looking into options to acquire funding for ADA
projects.  Discussion included future improvements and/or re-development of the city-owned parcel
of land adjacent to the southwest corne r of Holliday Park, near Lincolnway and Morrie Avenue.
There being no further comments or input and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor
Kaysen closed the hearing.  Mayor Kaysen noted the resolution affiliated with the hearing would be
discussed at the next Finance Committee meeting scheduled for July 7, 2014.
ORDINANCE - Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste fee schedule established by
Ordinance No. 4013 (postponed from 6/09/14).  Dino Moncecchi, Spartan Management, and George
Gliem, Cheyenne Landlord Association, spoke in opposition to the proposed fee increases, citing
concerns with multi-family units vs. single family residential rates, financial burden on tenants
residing in multi-family housing units, and cost of sanitation services vs. water and sewer services.
Mr. Moncecchi suggested that fee increases be “frozen”.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr.
Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Discussion included
the following topics: Consultant solid waste services rate study  and recommendations involving an
8% fee increase vs. staff’s proposed 3.4% increase, which is based on the Consumer Price Index
pursuant to ordi nance (provisions require staff to pre pare annual incr eases for g overning body
consideration based upon the Index); anticipated July time frame when the rate study information
will be provided to City Council members; funding needed for the Happy Jack Landfill closure and
expansion and Transfer Station expansion projects; increased routine operational and maintenance
costs; rates assessed to multi-family units such as duplexes and apartment buildings; current status
of the City’s application for a landfill (expansion) variance permit through the state; demonstrating
City efforts to fund the costs of solid waste operations relative to applying for state grants or loans;
content and comprehensiveness of elements included in the consultant’s study; meeting solid waste
community needs; City  Council decision to not approve r ate increa ses last y ear and cha nges
approved in assessing  commerci al account rates r esulting in re duced r evenue; Cit y Council’s
responsibility relative to determining fee increases and timing of same; concern with increased rates’
effect on individuals wi th fixed incomes, a nd waiting  to decide upon a ny incre ases until the
consultant’s study information, including staff comments and recommendations, have been received
and re viewed.  D uring discussion, J im Elias, Director of  Pu blic Works, provided detailed
information in response to queries, and Mayor Kaysen advised the pending Chamber of Commerce’s
development fee study will not specifically include all of the sanitation cost areas.  Mo tion to
approve failed ( City Clerk note: Passag e of a n ordinance on third and f inal reading requires the
affirmative vote of the majority (i.e. 6 “yes” votes) of the qualified members (10 members) of the
governing body).  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.
Voting “no” -- Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.
ORDINANCE - Amending various sections of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code
(UDC), to allow Service Uses, Animal Care - Hospital/Clinic in the CBD Central Business District
after conditional discretionary review.  Tiffany Healey, local veterinarian, spoke in support of the
ordinance.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor moved to approve on third and
final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present. (#4028)
ORDINANCE -  A mending O rdinance N o. 3 980 d efining th e te rms a nd c onditions of
employment for non-uniformed employees of the City of Cheyenne.  Mr. Brown moved to approve
on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Ms. Williams moved to amend by substitute,
seconded by Mr. Brown.  It was advised the purpose of the substitute is to remove wording affiliated
with the employee Wellness Program.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present.  Main motion as amended c arried.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the
governing body present. (#4029)
ORDINANCE - A mending Ordinanc e N o. 3981 defining  the terms and c onditions of
employment for sworn officers of the City of Cheyenne Police Department.  Mr. Brown moved to
approve on third and final r eading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Ms. W illiams moved to amend by
substitute, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  I t was advised the  purpose of  the substitute is t o remove
wording affiliated with the employee Wellness Program.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body present.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present. (#4030)
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land situated in
a portion of the N ortheast Quarter of the Nor thwest Quarter of Section 26, Township 14 North,
Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be known as the Del-Van Storage
Park Annexation (located at 5311 Dell Range Blvd. at the southwest corner of the intersection of
Gysel Place and Dell Range Blvd.).  Casey Palma, Steil Surveying Services, agent for the applicant,
advised a request was made at the Public Services Committee to postpone the item to allow for a
neighborhood meeting to be scheduled to discuss proposed development plans, noting an invitation
to the meeting would also be extended to governing body members.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve
on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Mr. Shanor moved to postpone to July 14,
2014, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present.  Mayor Kaysen advised the item would be reviewed at the next Public
Services Committee meeting scheduled for July 8, 2014.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from MU Mixed Use (County), MUB Mixed-Use Business

an invitation to the meeting would also be extended to governing body members. Mr. Shanor moved
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Mr. Shanor moved to postpone
to July 14, 2014, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present.  Mayor Kaysen advised the item would be reviewed at the next Public

 ORDINANCE - Amending Section 5.78.030(B)(5), Business License Required, of Chapter

    RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 July 14, 2014
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr.

Recognition o f “Cheyenne Wee kend”, be ing pr oduced by  C-SPAN and Charter
Communications, to be aired on Book TV and American History TV in the Fall of 2014.  Ashley
Hill, with the C-SPAN Cities Tour Project, advised a filming crew was in Cheyenne partnering with
Charter Communications to spotlight the community through coverage of various places, activities
and interviews.  She indicated the production would be aired on Book TV and American History TV
channels the weekend of September 6 and 7, 2014, and will also be available on C-SPAN’s website
for future vie wing.  Ms. Hill expressed appreciation to the City and Charter Communications for
welcoming them to the community and for their assistance.

of Tracts 323 and 324, Sunnyside Addition, 7th Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south
of and adjacent to Rock Springs Stree t, east of a nd adjacent to Pierce  Avenue, and we st of and
adjacent to Polk Avenue).  Jim Flesher, Planning  Service s Depa rtment, stated the pr oposed
annexation is pursuant to a petition submitt ed by Richardson Home s and that re quirements and
compliance of annexation conditions contained in state statutes had been met.  Upon inquiry, Mr.
Flesher advised the parcel is part of an existing county pocket.  Mayor Kaysen advised Agenda Item
#16 is a companion item.  Ther e being no further comments or input and purpose of the he aring
having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hea ring to d etermine whether compliance of annexation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land situated in the south ½ of
Tracts 105 and 106, Sunnyside Addition, Fourth Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the
northeast corner of the intersection of Monroe Avenue and Charles Street).  Logan Graves, Planning
and Development Office, stated the proposed annexation is pursuant to a petition submitted by the
William Pendley family and that requirements and compliance of annexation conditions contained
in state statutes had been met.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Graves confirmed the purpose of the annexation
request is for a single family home to connect to City water and sewer services.  There being no
further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the
hearing.  Mayor Kaysen advised Agenda Item #17 is a companion item.
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land situated in
a portion of the N ortheast Quarter of the Nor thwest Quarter of Section 26, Township 14 North,
Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be known as the Del-Van Storage
Park Annexation (located at 5311 Dell Range Blvd. at the southwest corner of the intersection of
Gysel Place and Dell Range Blvd.) (Postponed from June 23, 2014).  Arden and Sharon Carlberg,
and Kathy Scheurman, residents in the vicinity, spoke in opposition to a zone change affiliated with
the annexation, and indicated their desire that the property zone remain for residential use.  They
expressed concerns that if the property were developed for storage sheds it would negatively impact
property values in the neighborhood; that a proposed zone change to MUE (Mixed-Use Employment
Emphasis) “does not fit” the neighborhood, and commented on drainage concerns.  Ms. Carlberg
submitted a le tter in opposition (on file with Cit y Clerk’s off ice) and distributed photos of her
residential property adjacent to the parcel.  Casey Palma, Steil S urveying Services, agent for the
applicant, advised the annexation ordinance “is tied” to the zone change ordinance and preliminary
plat items (i.e. Agenda Items #10 and #23) and noted a neighborhood meeting was recently held to
discuss proposed development plans for the parcel in an effort to mitigate some of the neighbors’
concerns.  He provided information on drainage and storm water detention and engineering plans;
past proposed uses for the property that have not materialized, and the reasons for requesting the
MUE type zone classification.  He noted the current developers have structured their contract plans
to acquire the property based upon development of a storage park at the location and that the project
should have a low traffic effect on the neighborhood and that landscape buffering between residential
homes and the storage park would be provided.  Jerry Ciz, managing developer partner, advised they
had reviewed different development projects for the property and determined that storage units
would be the best use; provided information on the operational and management aspects, including
security and lig hting, planne d for the busine ss, and th e traffic asse ssment study ; advised a
neighborhood meeting had been held, and confirmed the developers have no interest in any other
type of development project for the property.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Up on inquiry, Dan White, City
Attorney, advised regardless of the ordinance content referring to the annexation as the Del-Van
Storage Park Annexation, the ordinance follows the same format as any other annexation ordinance
and if a nnexed the proper ty would become  subject t o the City’s jurisdiction and deve lopment
requirements.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Carlberg delineated where her residence is located relative to the
proposed annexation area.  Co mments followed regarding possible development of the property
through county approval if it is not annexed, and the motion before the governing body involving
annexation, not re-zoning.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with
the exception of Ms. Broyles, Mr. Luna and Ms. Williams voting “no”. 
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from MU Mixed Use (County), MUB Mixed-Use Business
Emphasis, and MR-1 Medium Density Residential-Established to MUE Mixed-Use Employment
Emphasis for Del-Van Storage Park, being a replat of all of Goodwater Addition, Goodwater Drive,
and Water Blue Lane and a portion of the Northeast Quarter of the Northwest Quarter of Section 26,
T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to Dell Range Blvd.,
east of and adjacent to Van Buren Ave.) (Postponed from 6/23/14).  Sha ron Carlberg spoke in
opposition to the proposed zone change, referencing her comments on the previous item.  Casey
Palma, Steil Surveying Services, agent for the applicant, confirmed the developers have no interest
in developing the prope rty for anything other than for stora ge units and indicate d if any  of the
required approvals through the City (i.e. annexation, re-zoning and final plat) were not received, the
developers would not file applica ble documents (a nnexation ordinance a nd/or final plat) f or
effectiveness and the project would not move forward.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second
reading, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Upon inquiry, Jerry Ciz, managing developer partner, advised a
study on storage unit needs in the community and particularly within the project area was done to
ascertain the public demand level.  Upon query, Mr. Ciz and Mr. Palma provided information on the
proposed storage unit “hig h tech” facility; unit sizes, and r easons for requesting the MUE zone
classification, which include better  aesthe tics and stree tscape amenities.  Discussion included
elements required and uses allowed under a MUE zone and differences between other zone types;
current mixed use zone for the property; buffering; traffic; drainage; conditional use approval for the
storage unit project that will be required through the Board of Adjustment; concerns involving
potential future uses of the property under a MUE zone if the storage unit project does not proceed,
and property owner and developer contractual arrangements.  During discussion and upon inquiry,
Brandon Cammarata, Planning and Development Director, provided information on development
of infill sites and advised review and consideration of other zoning classifications was conducted
with the MUE zone being supported by staff.  Mr. Cammarata also provided general information on
design standards a nd buffe ring require ments for other  zone ty pes pursua nt to th e Unifie d
Development Code (UDC).  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Dr.
Rinne, Mr. Shanor and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Broyles, Mr. Luna, Mr. Valdez and Ms.
Williams.  Mayor Kaysen advise d the will be re viewed a t the J uly 22, 2014 Public Services
Committee meeting.
 ORDINANCE - Amending Section 5.78.030(B)(5), Business License Required, of Chapter
5.78, Towing and Wrecker Recovery Operations, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of
the code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved
to a pprove on  se cond r eading, s econded b y Mr . Valdez.  Mo tion c arried.  Voting “y es” -- a ll
members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Amending various sections of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code
(UDC), allowing automatic irrigation to be optional for drought tolerant seeded grass landscape areas
located in the LI-Light Industrial and HI-Heavy Industrial Zoning districts as initiated from the 2014
Annual Revie w.  Mr. Shanor move d to approve  on second r eading, seconde d by  Mr. Allen.
Discussion included the following:  Comments in support as well as non-support of the proposed
change; option availability to new LI and HI zone developments or redevelopments if the ordinance
is approved; aesthetics; water conservation; concern that requests for other zones types to have the
same option may be received; weeds and natural grasses, and gateways into the community.  Upon
inquiry, Brandon Cammarata, Planning and Development Director, advised the option would be
applied to any development; provided information on when existing developments may be allowed
the option; indicated that weeds and run-off issues will still be the responsibility of the landowner,
and reviewed differences between the zones involving landscaping requirements for undeveloped
lands.  He stated the amendment proposed was a topic raised during the annual review process of
the Unified D evelopment Code ( UDC).  A list of L I and HI  zoned areas within the City was
requested to be provided to governing body members prior to third reading of the ordinance.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Broyles and
Ms. Williams voting “no”.

relative to ride owners and operators responsibilities, noting the ordinance primarily addresses ride
inspections.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.

d/b/a Red Lobster #0 639, 1923 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held by
GMRI, Inc. d/b/a Red Lobster #0639, 1923 Cheyenne, Wyoming).
Under other business, Pete Laybourn commented on the redevelopment of the downtown area
pursuant to the Historic Placemaking Plan, citing areas of disrepair and the need to make downtown
“sparkle”, particularly for the upcoming annual Frontier Days celebration.  During his comments he
submitted copies of a recent edition of a Denver, CO newspaper, noting similarities in downtown
improvement projects between the two cities, and expressed concerns with downtown landscaping,
the Downtown Development Authority, condition of alleys, and lack of coordination of services. 
There being  no further  business to co me before the g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 8:35 p.m.

 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
