MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-07-28 | CLIP_ID: 438 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 438

    RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 July 28, 2014
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms.
Williams.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  ( All agenda items listed wi th the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received. Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by Ms. Broyles
and Dr. Rinne, Agenda Item #18 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  M r. Shanor moved to
approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 14, 2014.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Mr. V aldez de clared a  conflict of  int erest ( business in terest i n v ouchers pa yable to R adio
Stations KRAE-AM and KYOY-FM) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve
payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote,
Mr. Valdez returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a retail liquor license
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Red Lobster Hospitality LLC d/b/a Red Lobster #0639,
1923 Dell Range Boulevard., Cheyenne, Wyoming  (License currently held by GMRI, Inc. d/b/a Red
Lobster #0639, 1923 Dell Range Boulevard, Cheyenne, Wyoming).  There being no comments or input
and the purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the
application to Finance Committee. 
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land situated in a
portion of the Northeast Quarter of the Northwest Quarter of Section 26, Township 14 North, Range
66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be  known as the Del-Van Storage Park
Annexation (located at 5311 Dell Range Blvd. at the southwest corner of the intersection of Gysel Place
and Dell Range Blvd.).  Jeff Fox, co-owner of the property, provided a brief history regarding their past
plans to develop the property to build 4-plexes and reasons why the development did not move forward.
He stated the owners decided to list the property for sale, and commented in support of the storage park
project a ffiliated w ith the  a nnexation.  Pu rsuant t o c onfirmation f rom Mayor Ka ysen th at th eir
comments could also address zoning concerns (i.e. affiliated with next agenda item), Sharon and Arden
Carlberg, adjacent property owners, spoke in opposition to the proposed zone change and indicated their
desire that the proper ty remain zoned for residential use.  Their c omments included the following
concerns: Impact on nearby property values; undesirable aspects of living next to storage shed units;
lack of an on-site manager, and the possibility of a change in project plans that may include a future re-
zoning request.  Ms. Carlberg submitted a handout in conjunction with her comments (on file with City
Clerk’s office).  Mr. Carlber g stated that as f ollow-up to information stated at a previous meeting
concerning a shortag e of available storage rental units in t he community, he had c ontacted several
businesses providing rental units and his research reflected a lack of evidence to support that a shortage
of available units exists.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor moved to approve on
third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body with the exception of Mr. Luna and Ms. Williams voting “no”.  (#4031)
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from MU Mixed Use (County), MUB Mixed-Use Business
Emphasis, and MR-1 Medium Density  Residential-Establish ed to MUE Mix ed-Use Employment
Emphasis for Del-Van Storage Park, being a replat of all of Goodwater Addition, Goodwater Drive, and
Water Blue Lane and a portion of the Northeast Quarter of the Northwest Quarter of Section 26, T.14N.,
R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to Dell Range Blvd., east of and
adjacent to Van Buren Ave.).  Mayor Kaysen advised the item was related to the previous agenda item.
Casey Palma, Steil Surveying Services, agent for the developers, noted discussions have been held with
vicinity landowners in an effort to address concerns; stated the MUE zone allows flexibility for use with
buffering protections, and advised approval from the Board of Adjustment will also be required to build
the storage units on the property.  Mr. Palma indicated that portions of the property are currently zoned
as MR-1, Mixed Use and MUB, and confirmed the developers have no interest in pursuing any other
type of development project for the property.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on third and final reading,
seconded by Mr. Mr. Cook.  Upon inquiry , Mr. Palma re viewed the ty pes of landsc ape ma terial
envisioned to be planted in the 15 ft. buffer around the perimeter area and discussions held wi th
adjacent property owners regarding the type of trees landowners would prefer.  Upon inquiry regarding
the shortage of storage units in the community, Jerry Ciz, co-developer, Del-Van Storage Park, advised
they will be developing  larger size units, which are  in sho rt demand, vs. smaller units which a re
available.  Upon inquiry, Jim Flesher, Planning and Development Office, clarified that, pursuant to the
zoning type, it would be a violation for persons to live in the units, and Mr. Palma explained that no
electricity/power in the units, other than exterior lighting, is proposed to avoid someone attempting to
live in or operate a business out of a unit.  Upon inquiry regarding potential loss of surrounding property
values, Mr. Ciz advised they have not seen any specific studies regarding either a decrease or increase
in property values regarding storage units near residential areas, noting storage units have marketability
qualities such as low traffic.  He explained an automated management system doesn’t mean that there
won’t be a property maintenance presence on the grounds.  Sharon Carlberg, adjacent property owner,
commented in opposition to the proposed zone change, referencing a national study she had read that
reflected a residential area next to a commercial industrial development decreases property values, and
expressed that in addition to the undesirable e ffect of viewing the storage units from her home, she
believes the units will affect her property’s value.  Motion ca rried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr .
Brown, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Luna,
Dr. Rinne and Ms. Williams. (#4032)
 ORDINANCE - Amending Section 5.78.030(B)(5), Business License Required, of Chapter 5.78,
Towing and Wrecker Recovery Operations, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the code
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body. (#4033)
ORDINANCE - A mending various sec tions o f the Cheyenne U nified De velopment Code
(UDC), allowing automatic irrigation to be optional for drought tolerant seeded grass landscape areas
located in the LI-Light Industrial and HI-Heavy Industrial Zoning districts as initiated from the 2014
Annual Review.  Speaking in support of the ordinance were:  Jean Vetter, who read a statement (on file
with City Clerk’s office) involving the positive aspects of native drought tolerant grasses and utilizing
alternate landscaping methods; John Volk, resident and local developer, who noted the cost of watering
large sodded grass areas, and Boyd Wiggam, Wyoming Liberty Group, commenting that the change
supports private property owner rights by giving landowners an option and promotes env ironmental
sustainability through water conservation measures.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on third and final
reading, seconded by Mr. B rown.  Upon inquiry , Brandon Cammarata, Planning a nd Development
Director, referred to a City map included in the staff report depicting the locations of LI and HI zoned
areas, and provided information on acreage sizes and locations generally of the two z ones involved,
noting that some LI zones are “sprinkled throughout” the City.  Comments followed in support of the
ordinance.  Motion  carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#4034)
ORDINANCE - Amending Sections 5.36.070, 5.36.080, 5.36.090, 5.36.110, and 5.36.120, of
Chapter 5.36, Circuses, Carnivals and Amusement Rides, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations,
of the c ode of the  City of Chey enne, Wyoming.  Mr. Shanor moved to appr ove on third and f inal
reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body. (#4035)
ORDINANCE - A mending Section 5.12.040(A)( 1)(B), I nsurance - Te rm of L icenses and
Permits - R enewal of Annual Licenses, of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Beverages, of Title 5, Business
Licenses and Regulations, of the code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mike Moser, Executive
Director, Wyoming State Liquor Association, commented on behalf of Laramie County liquor licensee
Association members, and also in support of the Wyoming TIPS (Training for Intervention ProcedureS)
Program.  Mr. Moser spoke in opposition to the proposed ordinance and provided information on the
TIPS program, advising of the training he and other volunteers provide throughout the state; on-line
training and affiliated fee for participants vs. benefits of no cost in-person classes; educational video
viewed by alcohol servers prior to employment duties, which is followed by the 4 hour TIPS training
class within 90 days of the server’s employment date as currently required by City Code, and reasons
for r etaining the cur rent 90 da y time frame  vs. the proposed c hange to 30 day s.  He re viewed
employment scenarios and difficulties for new-hire employees relative to acquiring the training in a
lesser time period; personal employment and management hardships that may occur if the change to 30
days was appr oved, and submitted (on f ile with City Clerk’s off ice) and discussed state  estimates
(percentages) of underage alcohol use and self purchase of alcohol data issued by the Substance Abuse
and Mental Health Services Administration (SAMHSA).  He expressed his belief that the majority of
underage individuals are not successfully purchasing alcohol themselves and are acquiring by other
means, and noted the positive percentage of City liquor licensees that met recent compliance checks and
cooperative teamwork efforts between licensees and local law enforcement.  Kevin Georges, loca l
liquor business owner/operator, reviewed concerns of bar owners and liquor establishment operators
with the proposed change to a 30 day time frame (from date of employment) in which servers would
need to acquire TIPS training, which could affect licensees, license status and continuing employment
of staff.  Mr. B rown moved to approve on se cond reading, seconded by Ms. Broy les.  Discussion
included the following topics: Allowing employers the time to evaluate new employees by retaining the
90 d ay t raining time frame; i mportance o f T IPS t raining; co ncern wi th al cohol s ales t o u nderage
individuals by servers who have not yet received training; number of state-wide TIPS trainers; proposed
ordinance not addressing the primary issue of underage drinking, and ordinance change burden on
licensees and management.  Motion to approve f ailed.  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Broyles and Dr. Rinne.
Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez, Ms. Williams and
Mayor Kaysen.  Under other business, Ms. Broyles provided information regarding Municipal Court
bond schedules that assist with holding servers and bar owners accountable, when violating laws
relating to alc ohol service  to undera ge in dividuals, as well as unde rage y ouths who are  caug ht
attempting to purchase alcohol.

zoning action i n re cognition t hat t here may be considerable c ost t o t he d eveloper i n p reparing a

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-08-11 | CLIP_ID: 440 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 440

    RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 August 11, 2014
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - M r. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms.
Williams.  Absent: Mr. Allen and Mr. Cook.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff,
City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received. Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.).  Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on July 28, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez and Mr. Brown each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers
payable to Radio Station KYOY-FM and Ameri-Tech HVAC Services respectively) and vacated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by
Ms. Williams.  Mo tion carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in
Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez returned to
Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hea ring to det ermine whether c ompliance of annexation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a portion of Tr acts 245, 246, 247 and 248,
Sunnyside Addition, 6th Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of the
intersection of Hayes Avenue and US Highway 30) to be known as Carter Cottages.  Logan Graves,
Planning and Development, stated the proposed annexation is pursuant to a petition for annexation
submitted by the property owner, Mousa Fino, through Brad Emmons, AVI, agent for the applicant,
and that requirements and compliance of annexation conditions contained in state statutes have been
met.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor
Kaysen closed the hearing.

Consideration of Bid #R-27-14 for furnishing two new cab and chassis units with 40 yard
front loader refuse collection bodies for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division (Solid Waste
Management Equipment Lease).  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works, explained the two units will be the
first step in automating commercial garbage collections; provided information on safety and reduced
costs of collection; reviewed bids submitted, which include trade-ins, and staff’s bid review process,
and stated staff supports the bid that most closely meets the needs of the City as identified in the bid
specifications.  Ernie Kois, Kois Brothers Equipment Company, submitted (on file with City Clerk’s
office) and discussed information in support of the bid submitted by Peterbilt of Wyoming with a
Heil manufactured body provided by Kois Brothers; reviewed similar equipment and body makes
utilized b y the  City  of  Gr and Junc tion, Col orado, a nd dis cussed it ems c ontained in the b id
specifications (i.e. overall height, length and steel composition) that were not met by the Heil product
and re asons fo r r equesting the  bid  wi th the He il b ody be  c onsidered.  Pa ul B utcher, L aBrie
Enviroquip Group, and Doug King and Dave King, Ameri-Tech Equipment Company, spoke in
support of the bid submitted by Peterbilt of Wyoming with a LaBrie manufactured body provided
by Am eri-Tech E quipment, no ting the bid co mplied w ith a ll o f t he City ’s re quested b id
specifications and advising that money had been expended in order  to meet the spec ifications as
written (spreadsheet showing costs submitted (on file with City Clerk’s office) by Doug King during
his c omments).  Sa ra Va squez, Pu rchasing Di vision, p rovided a  br ief b ackground o n th e bid
issuance, advertising, non-mandatory pre-bid meeting pro cess and bid amendment informa tion
issued affiliated with the bid.  Sh e explained minimum specifications are co ntained in the bid
document and pote ntial bidders have  a ti me fra me in which to re view and a dvise if there  are
concerns or questions concerning the minimum specifications.  Mr. Brown reported there was no
recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown moved to accept the bid from
Peterbilt of Wyoming, Cheyenne, [City Clerk’s note: with the LaBrie body provided by Ameri-Tech
Equipment Compa ny) per the re commendation of City  staff, in a n amount not to ex ceed
$496,184.00, including trade-ins, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Discussion topics included the integrity
of the  bid  pr ocess th rough a dherence to b id s pecifications; w arranty/guarantee of  the  un its;
lease/purchase arrangements; review of bids and recommendation by staff; training to be received
and affiliated cost, and storage  site of the units.  Vicki Nemec ek provided information on the
warranty time frame; lease/purchase financial arrangements to acquire the units as soon as they are
available; staff membe rs who re viewed a nd evalua ted bids rec eived, a nd type of tra ining a nd
affiliated costs, and advised the units will be stored at the Transfer Station.  During discussion on
the warranty topic, Mr. Kois and Mr. Doug King confirmed that their respective company will
comply with the warranty requirement.  Discussion continued on bid specifications and maintaining
integrity of the bid proc ess.  Upon inqu iry by Mayor Kaysen to members of  the governing body
present and there being no objection voiced, Mayor Kaysen queried Ms. Vasquez as to whether her
lack of presence at the pre- bid meeting, as mentioned during  Mr. Kois’ c omments, affected the
bidding procedure.  Ms. Vasquez responded that her absence bears no weight on the bid process.
Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek provided additional information on the lease/purchase arrangements
that will involve assessment of interest; mechanic training to be received and warranty time frame,
and advised staff w ill be looking at se tting up a de preciation fund in the future  for pur chases
pursuant to information contained in the recent rate study.  It was noted that historically the City has
entered into lease/purchasing agreements for Sanitation equipment as the cost is such that the City
hasn’t had the money to purchase the items outright.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-4-15 for furnishing one new 2014 - 2015 twin screw diesel-powered
cab and chassis for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division (Insurance and Miscellaneous Claims).
Motion (per Consent Ag enda) carried (accept bid from Peterbilt of Wy oming, Cheyenne, in a n
amount not to exceed $138,873.00).
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-6-15 for furnishing Police vehicles for the City of Cheyenne, Police

[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-1-15 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Fire and Rescue
Department, and Richardson Construction, Inc., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the Fire and Rescue/Police
Department Storage Building Project (Impact Assistance fund).  Motion (per  Consent Ag enda)

[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-2-15 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering

Markings Project (2011 - 2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not
to exceed $104,500.00). (#6223)
[CA] Consideration of B id #R-9-15 for f urnishing bulk ice control product t o the City  of
Cheyenne, Public Works Department (2011 - 2014 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-5-15 for furnishing two new 2014 - 2015 diesel-powered 3-wheeled
street sweepers with belt conveyor for the City of Cheyenne, Street and Alley  Division (2007-2011
and 2011-2014 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Red Lobster Hospitality
LLC d/b/a Red Lobster #0639, 1923 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming  (License currently held
by GMRI, Inc. d/b/a Red Lobster #0639, 1923 Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Motion (per Consent Agenda)
carried.
[CA] Annual Report to the City of Cheyenne regarding capital improvements made at the Airport
Golf Course as required by Lease Agreement.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (acknowledge
receipt).
Announcement was made of the Appointment of Kevin R. Williams to the International Fire
Code Board of Appeals, to be confirmed August 25, 2014. 
Announcement was made  of the Re- Appointments of Tere nce Roker, Philip Griggs, and
Mary (Aragon) Connor, to the Public Transit Advisory Board, to be confirmed August 25, 2014. 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held August 25, 2014 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Cham bers, 2101 O’Neil Ave nue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to re ceive public input on an
application requesting the establishment of a Residential Critical Traffic and Parking Area in the
vicinity of the Cheyenne Regional Medical Center.
Under other business:
Rona Bartos commented briefly in support of the petition submitt ed by her and neighbors
requesting the critical parking area near the hospital.  Mayor Kaysen explained that the previous item
on the agenda (refer to minutes above) pertained to the application/petition request and reiterated that
a Public Hearing on the request would be held August 25, 2014 at 6:00 p.m.
There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning bod y, the meeting wa s
officially adjourned at 6:55 p.m.

 Carol Intlekofer, MMC

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-08-25 | CLIP_ID: 442 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 442

     RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 August 25, 2014
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr.
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present:  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer,
and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Allen moved to approve, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Mayor Kaysen noted that two Consent
Agenda items were referrals to Committee of the Whole, which would meet September 3, 2014 at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 11, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Shanor  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive public input on an application requesting
the establishment of a Residential Critical Traffic and Parking Area in the vicinity of the Cheyenne
Regional Me dical Ce nter.  Ma rk Es cobedo, T raffic En gineer, a dvised th e pu blic hearing is i n
conjunction with the application that had been submitted and that application requirements had been
met per City Code.  Rona Bartos reviewed reasons for the application being submitted and read a
statement concerning issues residents in t he neighborhood are experiencing due to the congested
parking conditions around the hospital (medic al center) and use of on- street parking spaces by
hospital employees, visitors and vendors.  Her comments included the following topics: Measures
the hospital has taken to assist with relief to the neighborhood; debris being deposited on private
properties; blockage of drive-ways and unavailable parking for residents, visitors, and home delivery
services; concern that a 2-hour parking limit as provided under establishment of a critical traffic and
parking area will not alleviate the overall parking situation, and enforcement concerns.  Ms. Bartos
recommended that residential parking through a permitting process be implemented without the 2-
hour restriction.  Discussion included enforcement of the area for compliance, particularly after work
hours and on weekends; residential permit parking relative to the establishment of a critical traffic
and parking area and the current 2-hour parking limit time frame per City Code, and potential future
ordinance cha nge re garding the time fra me.  Upon inquiry , Mr. Escobe do provided g eneral
information on signage, monitoring and enforcement.  Pursuant to inquiry concerning the retroactive
effect that a future ordinance changing the 2-hour time frame would have on the current application
if approved under current 2-hour parking limit provisions, Dan White, Cit y Attorney, responded
research would need to be  conducted on the t opic.  Ms. Will iams submitted a r esolution to Mr.
White for legal review.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having
been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the application to Pub lic S ervices
Committee.

Williams moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointment).
Re-Appointments of Terence Roker, Philip Griggs and  Mary (Aragon) Connor, to the Public
Transit Advisory Board.  Ms. Williams moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not

Announcement was made that due to the Labor Day Holiday on Monday, September 1, 2014,
regularly sche duled City Council Comm ittee Mee tings will instead be he ld in the Municipal
Building, Room 122, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance Committee
will meet Tuesday, September 2, 2014 a t 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee will meet
Wednesday, September 3, 2014 at Noon.
Under other business, Tom Lindsey, reviewed past history involving the vacant downtown
“hole” and expressed his opinion on the need for re-development of the site.  He proposed a multi-
purpose building with an underground parking garage be built on the property, and offered his
suggestions on various types of businesses and uses that could be housed in the building, such as a
restaurant, tourism services, residential units and a roof-top open social space area.  He stated his
belief that a multi-purpose building would generate revenue.
There being  no furth er business t o come be fore the g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 6:46 p.m.

 Carol Intlekofer, MMC

guide to the community ’s goals of managing stormwater in the L ower Capitol Basin, mitiga ting
brownfields, creating meaningful public spaces, and facilitating private investment in the near west
side of downtown Cheyenne.  Mr. Shanor moved to adopt, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Mr. Shanor
moved to amend by including staff adjustments as indicated on page 2 of the staff summary report
involving the following, seconded by Ms. Broyles: Executive Summary (page 6); 4.5.4 Investment
Supportive En vironment; 4. 4.1 U rban Re newal A uthority; 4. 5.8 ( New St rategy) H istoric
Preservation, and 4.7.1 District Plan.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen confirmed use of sales tax funds
toward the project will be pursuant to the 1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax ballot question
approved by voters.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5617)
  RESOLUTION - Building Cheyenne’s f uture b y r eaffirming th e Ci ty o f Cheyenne’s
commitment to ethical principles and efficiency in government.  Sara Vasquez, President, Cheyenne
Public Employees Association (CPEA), commented in opposition to the resolution, noting that ethics
principles are already in place f or City employees.  Repor ting for Committee of the Whole, Dr.
Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone for two weeks (to
Sept. 22, 2014), seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Discussion included comments in support as well as non-
support of postponement; lack of City Attorney’s approval stamp on the document; existing ethics
code for employees, and City Personnel Rules and Reg ulations, pending revisions and a pproval
procedure pursuant to the Wyoming Administrative Procedure Act (WAPA).  During comments, Dr.
Rinne raised a point of order that comments were not germane to the motion on the floor with Mayor
Kaysen concurring that discussion should pertain to the motion to postpone.  Upon inquiry, Mayor
Kaysen advised if the motion was approved the item would go back to Committee of the Whole for
further review and recommendation and then return to the governing body for action, and confirmed
that the rules and regulations are in the process of being revised and approval of revisions by the
governing body is not required unde r the WAPA.  Mot ion to postpone failed ( due to a tie vote) .
Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr.
Brown, Ms. Br oyles, Mr. C ook, Dr. Rinne and May or Kaysen.  Discussion on the main motion
followed a nd included the f ollowing topics: Curre nt Code of Ethics; int ent of the r esolution;
proposed revisions to the Personnel Rules and Regulations, and awareness of problems the City of
Casper is currently experiencing.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen clarified revisions to the 2008 rules
and regulations edition invol ve compliance areas with feder al, state a nd local laws; ve rified the
City’s Risk Safety Manual was not in the process of being updated, and explained the c urrent
administrative  complaint process that may be utilized by the public.  Upon query to City Council
members by Mayor Kaysen and there being no objection raised, Mayor Kaysen provided additional
information re garding content conta ined in the rules a nd regulations with regar d to retaliatory
behavior and disciplinary steps, and state s tatute provisions on ethics and sta ndards.  F ollowing
further discussion, motion to adopt failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Luna,
Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no”-- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Dr.
Rinne and Mayor Kaysen. 
RESOLUTION - To inc rease economic development by  reorganizing the Planning and
Development Offices as ind ependent entities. Speaking in support of the re solution were:  Joe
Patterson, Patterson Construction and President, Southeast Wyoming Home Builders Association;
Noel Gr iffith, local arch itect; Tammy Gra nt, President, Chey enne Re altors Association; L inda
Weppner, business owner; B ill Edwa rds, Edwards Construction; F rank Cole, United W estern
Corporation; Boyd Wiggam, Wyoming Liberty Group; John Volk, l ocal developer; Jim Weaver,
local realtor; John Mason, business owner; Dale Steenbergen, Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, and
Shawn Ajdari, local developer.  Topics discussed included the following: Expediting development
procedures and workflow; easier and more efficient development processes; designating staff to
facilitate and navigate customers through development procedures; separation of offices to provide
consistency and ac countability; meeting  curr ent re sidential housi ng needs in a timely  manner ;
suggestion of also separating the Building Safety functions into its own department entity; “one stop
shop” concept for customers a nd meaning  of the ter m; differe nces be tween pla nning a nd
development functions; production performance measures; time involved in staff and other agencies’
review process; costs t o developers; use of technology and software; small business concerns;
Chamber of Commerce members’ support (Mr. Steenbergen submitted documentation of members’
signatures in favor of the resolution -- on file with City Clerk’s office); difference between economic
activity vs. development; interested parties involvement in development process and review and
streamlining of same, and examples of community business developments and time frames involved
in reviewing, inspecting and approving applications.  Jan Nelson-Schroll expressed her opinion that
communication and participation by residential neighborhoods should occur relative to proposed
developments or re-developments affecting their areas.  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.
Shanor.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by adding the following sentence at the end of Section (5)
under the Now, Therefore, Be It Resolved clause, seconded by Ms. Williams: “The Council also
hereby recommends that immediately after the adoption of this resolution the Mayor seek input from
the Council and interested stakeholders, including but not limited to, the Greater Cheyenne Chamber
of Commerce, the Southeast Wy oming Homebuilders Association, and the Chey enne Board of
Realtors, as to how to best ef fectuate the intent of this re solution.”  Speaking  in favor of  the
amendment were: Mr. Steenbergen, Mr. Edwards and Ms. Nelson-Schroll.  During discussion by
governing body members, it was confirmed that intent of the wording in Section (5), current as well
as the proposed amendment, was to also include City staff participation relative to garnering input
and attending related meetings, and comments in support as well as non-support of the amendment
were made.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the
exception of Mr. B rown voting “no.”  Discussion followed on the  main motion as amended a nd
included the f ollowing topics: Uncertainty whether “splitting” the  offices would result in better
efficiency; concept of a “one stop shop”; addressing concerns legislatively vs. administratively;
positive comments about current planning and development City staff, and comment that wording
of the approved amendment may be all that was needed vs. the resolution.  During discussion and
upon inquiry regarding how City staff and the administration felt about the proposed organizational
splitting of the offices, Mayor Kaysen provided information on several administrative operational
and procedural changes that have occurred over the past few years as well as those currently being
worked upon; noted that staff currently provides “facilitator” assistance for customers; stated that
compliance with federal, state and local codes must be met; ac knowledged procedures should be
reviewed, and indicated that it is unknown whether separating the offices will work.  Ms. Broyles,
seconded by Mr. Cook, moved to amend by  revising the wording in Section (3) under the Now,
Therefore, Be It Resolved clause to read as follows: “The Development Office will employ and/or
appoint an advocate for private citizens to assist with the City’s regulatory process in order to speed
project approval; and ...”.  Joe Gallagher expressed his belief that the Development office needs a
facilitator, not an advocate.  Comments followed in support as well as non-support of the proposed
amendment.  Discussion topics included:  Lack of timeliness of development processes affecting
contractors, developers and residents; micro-managing; concerns that have been expressed about
application reviews and inspec tions resulting in additional re quirements, and customer  service.
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of
Mr. Brown voting “no.”  Discussion continued on the main motion as twice amended and included
references to previous comments; consistency and transparency; procedural challenges; facilitating
community development; support for  new de velopment director staff position as an appointee;
micro-managing, and governing body’s statutory authority to deal with departments and personnel.
Main motion as twice ame nded carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr.
Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez, Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown and
Dr. Rinne. (#5618)
Following a brief recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:40 p.m.

Application requesting the establishment of a Residential Critical Traffic and Parking Area
in the vicinity of the Cheyenne Regional Medical Center.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve, seconded
by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Shanor moved to postpone for two weeks (to Sept. 22, 2014).  Motion to postpone
died due to the lack of a second to the motion.  Comments followed in support of the application to
offer the neighborhood relief from parking congestion conditions.  Main motion carried.  V oting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Request to approve change of location of additional dispensing room for City of Cheyenne

     RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY

Office of City Clerk
 September 22, 2014
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne (arriving at 6:04
p.m.), Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Al so present: Kristy Anderson, Deputy City
Clerk; Lois Huff, City  Treasurer, and Da n White, City Attorney.  The pledg e of a llegiance was
recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be

Broyles moved to approve remaining consent agenda items, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.

Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers payable to Ameri-

Following approval of vouchers, Mayor Kaysen noted for those present in the audience, Agenda Item
#12(a), Preliminary Plat for Thomas Heights, would have no further discussion on it at the meeting
since it was approved on the Consent Agenda.
ORDINANCE  - Vacating a portion of Alexander Avenue and 13th Street, adjacent to Blocks
444, 445, 452 and 453, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located south of E. Lincolnway, west
of Dunn Avenue). Reporting for Public Service s Committee, Mr. Shanor  moved to approve  on
second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carrie d.  Voting “ yes” -- all members of the
governing body.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from MR-1 Medium Density Residential-Established to
MUB Mixed-Use B usiness Emphasis for L ots 7 and 8, Bloc k 509, Original Cit y, Chey enne,
Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of the intersection of W. 10th Street and Bent Avenue).
Mr. S hanor m oved t o a pprove o n s econd r eading, s econded b y M s. W illiams.  U pon i nquiry,

and explained there  are  mix ed uses in the a rea, including r esidential. He  further  advised that
notification to residents in the area was provided.  Upon inquiry, Gary Grigsby, Western R&D, agent
for applicant, clarified there isn’t a curre nt plan to erect a  metal building.  He  advised with the
exception of one residential property, the entire block is zoned MUB, therefore, the applicant felt
it was best for this property to also be zoned MUB.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body.

according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
(#5619)

of Transportation grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6240)
[CA] Right-of-Way Encroachment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and WILLCO IX
Development, LLLP, to construct a loading and unloading lane and island on Pioneer Avenue in
front of the future Hilton Garden Inn.   Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6241) 
Project Development and Administration Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the
Children’s Museum of Cheyenne, for development of a Children’s Museum. Tom Lindsey, spoke
in opposition to the agreement and expressed concerns regarding potential costs to taxpayers. Amy
Surdam, President, Board of Directors, Children’s Museum, spoke in fa vor of the agreement and
explained what the museum project will entail, its mission and the positive impact it will have on
the City.  Jim Weaver, #1 Properties, agent for Outback Investments, owner of the Hynds Building,
provided a  handout (on file w ith Ci ty Clerk’s Of fice) and state d that they  believe the cur rent
agreement poses a risk to the Hynds Building owners utilizing the corridor, and requested a possible
postponement to address a use agreement for the corridor. Discussion continued on negotiations for
use of the corridor.  Additional individuals who commented were:  Meredith Asay, member of Board
of Directors of Children’s Museum; Buck Holmes, and Hans Seitz, Pres., Downtown Development
Authority. Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded b y Mr.
Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Surdam explained that they have been in contact with local architects
who have developed conceptual drawings, and have reviewed the planned space of the museum,
including HVAC and elevators located on east side of the building that abuts the Hynds Building.
Mr. Brown moved to postpone f or 2 weeks (to October 13, 2014).  Du e to lack of a se cond, the
motion to postpone died.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
(#6242)
[CA] Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and Recreation Department, and
Brian Chalstrom, d/b/a Putt Hutt , for pr emises used a s a miniature g olf course in L ions Park --
referred to Finance Committee.

recycling services (Solid Waste Fund) -- referred to Finance Committee.

Constructors of Wy oming, L LC, establishing the G uaranteed Ma ximum Price (GMP) for  the
Cheyenne Public Safe ty Center Projec t (1% Specif ic Purpose Sales &  Use Ta x).  Mot ion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6115)
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 6151 be tween the  City  of Chey enne,
Engineering Department, and Short Elliott  Hendrickson, Inc., for the 19th Street Rehabil itation
Project (2011 - 2014 1% Sales Tax, BOPU Funds) -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 6190 between the City of Cheyenne and Deep
Green Energy, LLC, for conditions requiring additional work in the George Cox and Jack R. Spiker
parking facilities (Impact Assistance funds) -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Authorization of single source quote from Dant Clayton Corporation, Parker, Colorado, for
purchase of blea chers at Powers B aseball Field, Parks and Rec reation Depar tment (Special
Distribution funds).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted in the amount of $73,641.00).
[CA] Authorization of single sour ce quote  from AquaClimb/Pyramide U SA, I nc., F rederick,
Maryland, for a  purcha se ag reement to acquire  a Glac ier Climb W all, Parks and Rec reation
Department, Cheyenne Aquatic Center (Recreation Program funds).  Motion (per Consent Agenda)
carried (accepted in an amount not to exceed $37,865.00).  
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-5-15 for a contract for the HR Ranch Road Greenway Gap project
between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and Recreation Department, and Simon Contractors, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (Development Impact Funds - Parks Infrastructure) -- referred to Finance Committee.
Request to approve change of location of additional dispensing room for City of Cheyenne
resort liquor licensee, The Plains Hotel Co., LLC d/b/a The Plains Hotel, located a t 1600 Central
Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Announcement was made that the Laramie County Republican Party will be sponsoring a
Calcutta to be held Friday, October 10, 2014 beginning at 6:30 p.m. at the Old West Museum, 4610
Carey Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, in conjunction with the Red State Rendezvous (Proceeds to
benefit Laramie County Republican Party).
Under Other Business:
Major Dorr spoke about development in different areas of Cheyenne and expressed concerns
regarding the need for a grocery store in West Cheyenne.  Upon inquiry, Matt Ashby, Planning and
Development Director, explained that with the West Edge development project, prospects for any
type of a grocery store would be on a smaller scale. 
Ms. Williams moved to reconsider the vote taken at the Sept. 8, 2014 City Council meeting
for an “Application requesting the establishment of a Residential Critical Traffic and Parking Area
in the vicinity of the Cheyenne Regional Medical Center,” seconded by Mr. Allen.  It was advised
the purpose of the motion to reconsider was to amend the application to extend the area’s boundaries
as recommended by the City Traffic Engineer.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body.  Mayor Kaysen advised the main motion to approve the Residential Critical Traffic
and Parking Area was now back before the governing body.  Deputy City Clerk Kristy Anderson read
the entitlement into the record as follows: “Application requesting the establishment of a Residential
Critical Traffic and Parking Area in the vicinity of the Cheyenne Regional Medical Center.”  Ms.
Williams moved to postpone to Oct. 13, 2014, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion to postpone carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Mayor Kaysen announced due to postponement,
the item w ill be  re viewed a t th e ne xt Pub lic Se rvices Co mmittee me eting wh ich w ill be  he ld
Tuesday, Oct. 7, 2014 at noon, Room 122, of the Municipal Building.  
There bein g no further business to come  befor e the g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 7:42 p.m. 
 Submitted by,
 Kristy B. Anderson, CMC
 Deputy City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-10-13 | CLIP_ID: 449 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 449

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
October 13, 2014
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n Council C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne (arriving at 6:12
p.m.), Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois
Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Shanor moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 22, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Shanor each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers
payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services and First Judicial District Attorney’s Office respectively)
and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Allen moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Discussion followed on a vouche r payable to Gregory Allen Crisp for
services involving hosting of the Mayor’s Council for Persons with Disabilities (MCPD) website.
Comments included concerns that MCPD Board members had not approved the services and did not
have a dministrative ed iting r ights to the site, and tha t cer tain content on the we bsite was not
applicable to the MCPD.  Mayor Kaysen provided information relative to the formation and structure
of the MCPD, noting that monies raised by  the MCPD were  being utilized for payment of the
voucher; advised that Mr. Crisp had agreed to provide website services a few years ago when he was
a member of the B oard, and stated he ha d not received anything official from the MCPD Board
indicating Mr. Crisp’s servic es we re be ing disc ontinued.  Ms . Wil liams moved to amend by
removing the voucher for Mr. Crisp from the payables list.  Discussion followed on the time-frame
of services provided relative to the voucher; non-MCPD information on the website, and allowing
time for communication with the MCPD Board prior to acting on the voucher.  Motion to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers with the
exception of Dr. Rinne “abstaining” (due to his joining the meeting during discussion on the motion
to amend).  Request was made to provide Council members with an update on the Christensen Road
Overpass Project pursuant to 2 affiliated vouchers on the payables list.  Mayor Kaysen advised he
would request the MPO (Metropolitan Planning Organization) Director to provide an update.  Main

Application requesting the establishment of a Residential Critical Traffic and Parking Area
in the vicinity  of the Chey enne Re gional Medica l Ce nter.  Mr. Shanor moved to a pprove by
including the  e xpansion of  the  Cr itical T raffic and Parking a rea a s r ecommended b y the  City
Engineering Department, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Clarification of the map reflecting the expanded
area was provided.  Upon inquiry , Mark Esc obedo, Traffic Engineer, advised the intent of the
Critical Traffic and Parking Area ordinance is to provide r elief to residents involving residential
congested parking areas, and advised the parking restrictions will not apply to business properties
within the area.  H e explained many of the businesses may already have time limited parking
restrictions in place, and provided information on residential parking permits and fine amounts for
violations of the restric ted parking time limi ts.  I n response  to comment conce rning whether
Community Service Officers with the Police Department could assist with enforcement coverage,
Mayor Kaysen advised he would communicate with the Police Chief regarding that possibility for
patrol assistance.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Announcement was made of the Re-Appointment of Judith A. Lane, to th e C heyenne
Housing Authority Board, to be confirmed October 27, 2014.
Announcement was made of a public hearing to be held October 27, 2014, at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’N eil Avenue, to determine whe ther compliance of annexation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land situated in a portion of the
N.E. 1/4 of the S.E. 1/4 of Section 20, T.14N., R.66W., 6  P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming, to be knownth
as Scenic Development (located west of and adjacent to Converse Avenue, north of Dell Range
Boulevard).
Announcement was made of a public hearing to be held October 27, 2014, at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, to receive public comment regarding the intent of the
City of Cheyenne to submit an application for funding to the Wyoming Business Council’s Business
Ready Communi ty, Communi ty Enhancement Grant Prog ram, for  the Cahill Park Universa l
Playground Project.
Under other business, Jeffrey Dierksen addressed the governing body with tr affic a nd
pedestrian safety concerns he and others in his neighborhood have regarding the speed limit on a
portion of Pinto L ane.  He referenced a  petition (on file with City  Clerk’s off ice) signe d by
neighborhood supporters in the area requesting re-designation of Pinto Lane as a local street, instead
of a collector street;  reducing the speed limit to 25 mph, and installing speed humps and a pedestrian
crossing with signa ge.  Mayor Kaysen acknowledged receipt of the  petition and explained the
process the City utilizes for requests of this type, particularly when an area of concern is close to
schools; advised of the City ’s Neighborhood Transportation Plan and MPO Corri dor Plans, and
stated the concerns presented would be reviewed and analyzed by applicable City staff.
Bart Stolp also addressed the governing body with traffic and pedestrian safety concerns
involving a por tion of Van B uren Avenue between Dell Range Blvd. and Highway 30, which
includes the Dildine Elementary School area.  He referenced a letter he had previously submitted to
the City (on file with City Clerk’s office) that included signatures of neighbors in the area in support
of reviewing the concerns presented by Mr. Stolp.  He commented on high traffic speeds; suggested
flashing lights be utilized near the  school for student/pedestrian safety, and requested signage be
located more north of the school as well as the installation of speed humps.  Mayor Kaysen advised
applicable City staff will review the concerns presented and will also communicate with the school
district regarding the issues that Mr. Stolp had raised.
There being  no furth er business t o come be fore the g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 6:55 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 October 27, 2014

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-10-27 | CLIP_ID: 451 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 451

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
October 27, 2014
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n Council C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and
Ms. Williams.  Ab sent: Mr. Allen.  Al so present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Ms. Williams moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 13, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Service s) and va cated Counc il Chambers.  Mr. Valde z moved to approve pa yment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hea ring to determine w hether compliance of anne xation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land situated in a portion of the
Northeast Quarter of the Southeast Qua rter of Section 20, T.14N., R.66W. , 6  P.M., Cheyenne,th
Wyoming, to be known as Scenic Development (located west of and adjacent to Converse Avenue,
north of Dell Range Boulevard).  Jim Flesher, Planning Services Department, stated the proposed
annexation is p ursuant t o a  pe tition re questing a nnexation su bmitted b y Scenic De velopment,
property owner, and that requirements and compliance of annexation conditions contained in state
statutes had been met.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having
been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive public comment regarding the intent of the
City of Cheyenne to submit an application for funding to the Wyoming Business Council’s Business
Ready Com munity, Co mmunity En hancement Grant P rogram, f or the  Ca hill Pa rk Un iversally
Accessible Playground Project.  Cindy Schneider, Grants Manager, advised the project will include
the de molition a nd removal of  c urrent p layground e quipment a t th e pa rk a nd e xplained th at a
universally acc essible playground has f eatures that allow a ccessibility and use by  all able a nd
disabled children, adults and ca regivers.  She re viewed the  purpose a nd require ments of the
Community Enhancement Grant Program, noting that Cheyenne does not currently have this type
of playground in the community; advised the project is estimated to cost $420,000.00 with the grant
request in the amount of $210,000.00, and stated the City will match the grant request amount
through in-kind services, community facility fees and a recreation mill grant that has already been
approved for the pr oject.  Ms. S chneider also reviewed the time fra mes involved with the g rant
process.  Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, distributed a project summary sheet (on file
with City Clerk’s off ice); provided a br ief history of ef forts made toward implem entation of a
universally accessible playground, and e xplained why Cahill P ark was selected as the loc ation.
Speaking in support of the playground project were Monica Puente, former Leadership Cheyenne
participant, and JoAnne Weigand and Anne Picot, Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities.
Comments included whether Cahill Park was the best location for the playground.  There being no
further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the
public hearing.
ORDINANCE - A mending Section 6.04.020, Definitions, of Chapter 6.04, Definitions,
Administration and Enforcement; Creating a new Section 6.08.190, Community Cat Initiative, of
Chapter 6.08, Animal Care and Control Regulations Generally; and Amending Section 6.20.020,
Animal Impoundment, of Chapter 6.20, Impoundment, of Title 6, Animals, of the Code of the City
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mayor Kaysen requested Ms. Broyles, as sponsor of the ordinance, provide
an explanation of the purpose of the ordinance.  Ms. Broyles advised the ordinance would allow for
a more effective approach of addressing the community’s current homeless cat population and will
involve a humane  proce ss of trapping , neuter ing, a nd rele asing the cats, resulting  in a lower
population of community cats.  Britney Wallesch, Executive Director, Black Dog Animal Rescue,
spoke in support of the or dinance and use of humane “trap and return” programs.  Reporting for
Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms.
Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
 
ORDINANCE - A mending Section 12 .04.010, Obstructions on Streets, and Sec tion
12.04.080, Construction and Repair - Permits Required - Fees - Exception, of Chapter 12.04, Streets,
Sidewalks and Driveways Generally, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Ms. Broyles, as sponsor of the ordinance, briefly explained the
ordinance would provide for clarification regarding the placing of storage containers and roll-offs
within City rights-of-way and reviewed elements of the proposed ordinance involving a permit
process (with a fee); landscaping projects/materials; time limits, and notification (permit posting)
requirements.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on second reading,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry regarding the proposed 72 hour time limit for obstructions
involving landscaping materials, Rob Geringer, City Engineer, advised the time limit will provide
for proper protection for sediment control and run-off concerns.  He advised this could be construed
as an e nforcement issu e if landscaping obstructions ex tend beyond the time limit .  Discussion
followed on the length of time needed to complete certain landscaping projects.  Dr. Rinne moved
to amend par agraph C.1. of Sec tion 12.04.010 to ins ert the w ords “without a permit” so the
subsection would re ad as f ollows, seconded by  Mr. Cook: “The pe riod of obstruction shall not
exceed seventy-two (72) hours without a permit;”.  Comments followed on the initial intent of the
ordinance, which does not have a permit process applicable to landscaping materials; reasonable time
limit regarding landscaping materials as obstructions, and concerns with displaying a permit if a
permit is required for landscaping materials.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Broyles,
Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor a nd Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Mr. Luna, Mr.
Valdez and Mayor Kaysen. [City Clerk note:  Announcement of voting results reflected the motion
failed, however, upon subsequent review of votes cast (following closure of the meeting), it was
confirmed the motion to amend carried.]  Ms. Broyles moved to amend by substitute (dated Oct. 27,
2014), seconded by Mr. Brown.  Changes contained in the proposed substitute were reviewed and,
upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, clarified language shown as a “strike through” in Section
12.04.080 B in the substitute had a lso been include d in the  initial ordinance.  Motion to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Luna
voting “no”. [City Clerk note: The motion to amend by  substitute ordinance replaced the initial
ordinance, as amended.]  Discussion followed and included comments in support as well a s
opposition to the ordinanc e.  Topics include d: Current City  Code obstructions  in rights-of-way
restrictions; enforcement concerns; permit/fee requirement; length of the proposed ordinance and
various violation sections, and purpose of the ordinance, which is to address how long storage
containers and landscaping materials may be located on streets (public rights-of-way).  Upon inquiry,
Mr. White advised the ordinance structure format was based on an ordinance considered by City
Council a few years ago, and at the current time it is unlawful to have obstructions of this type in the
public rights-of-way.  He explained the ordinance would provide for the obstructions under certain
conditions, and include affiliated violations, and would allow people to use rights-of-way for private
uses on a limited basis.  Motion to approve on second reading, as amended by substitute ordinance,
failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne, and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” --  Mr.
Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.

[CA] RESOLUTION - Re-appropr iating the Fiscal Year 2015 budg et of the City  of Cheyenne

(#5622)
RESOLUTION - G ranting an exception for de velopment of small waste water treatment
systems pursuant to the 201 Agreement for Cheyenne Power Park, a planned unit development,
located generally within the NW1/4 of Section 29, T.13N., R.66W., of the 6  P.M., Laramie County,th
Wyoming.  Ga ry Hickm an, Chey enne-Laramie County  Hea lth Depar tment, Division of
Environmental Health, briefly explained the 201 Agreement and provisions within the Agreement
for granting an exception to requirements as affiliated with the resolution.  James Perrott McNeil
spoke in opposition to development of any land or any zone changes on Union Pacific (railroad)
property.  Casey  Palma, Steil  Survey ing Ser vices, a gent for the  proper ty owner , ref erenced
information that had been previously provided regarding approval of the hardship exception request
to allow for 11 small septic system permits (one per lot) to be issued for the Cheyenne Power Park,
located south of Cheyenne, for development purposes, and requested approval of the resolution.  Mr.
Shanor reported there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Mr.
Shanor m oved to  a dopt, se conded b y Mr. Luna.  M r. Sha nor m oved to  a mend by  su bstitute
resolution, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  It was advised the proposed substitute addresses concerns that
had been raised relating to “land locking” growth of the City by adding Condition c. under Section
2 of the resolution to read as follows: “If, and when, conditions for annexation exist for any of the
properties governed by the Cheyenne Power Park PUD, the owner or owners of each such property
shall seek a nnexation int o the municipal boundar ies of the City  of Chey enne.”.  Mr. Hickman
explained that whenever permits are issued it includes wor ding that when City  sewer services
become available, the landowner would be required to hook-up.  Discussion followed and included
the following topics: Whether the amendment, the exception, and 201 Agreement generally “runs”
with the property or with the current or future owner of the property; party responsible for acquiring
the State Engineer’s permit to drill water wells; lot sizes relative to septic systems; whether permits
are recorded with the County Clerk; annexation; property covenants; maintenance of 201 permits
through t he Environmental He alth office ; potential scena rios if wells/septic sy stems fail, and
enforcement of the 201 Agreement.  Upon inquiry during discussion, Dan White, City Attorney, and
Mr. Hickman provided information available relating to permits; hardship exemptions; septic system
criteria; whether permits “run” with the prope rty itself or with the prope rty owner, and the 201
Agreement, which deals with septic/sewage systems, not water wells.  Motion to amend car ried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Williams
voting “no”.  Main motion, as amended by substitute, carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present with the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”. (#5623)

MEETING: City Council Meeting (Copy) | DATE: 2014-10-27 | CLIP_ID: 453 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 453

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
October 27, 2014
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n Council C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and
Ms. Williams.  Ab sent: Mr. Allen.  Al so present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Ms. Williams moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 13, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Service s) and va cated Counc il Chambers.  Mr. Valde z moved to approve pa yment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hea ring to determine w hether compliance of anne xation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land situated in a portion of the
Northeast Quarter of the Southeast Qua rter of Section 20, T.14N., R.66W. , 6  P.M., Cheyenne,th
Wyoming, to be known as Scenic Development (located west of and adjacent to Converse Avenue,
north of Dell Range Boulevard).  Jim Flesher, Planning Services Department, stated the proposed
annexation is p ursuant t o a  pe tition re questing a nnexation su bmitted b y Scenic De velopment,
property owner, and that requirements and compliance of annexation conditions contained in state
statutes had been met.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having
been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive public comment regarding the intent of the
City of Cheyenne to submit an application for funding to the Wyoming Business Council’s Business
Ready Com munity, Co mmunity En hancement Grant P rogram, f or the  Ca hill Pa rk Un iversally
Accessible Playground Project.  Cindy Schneider, Grants Manager, advised the project will include
the de molition a nd removal of  c urrent p layground e quipment a t th e pa rk a nd e xplained th at a
universally acc essible playground has f eatures that allow a ccessibility and use by  all able a nd
disabled children, adults and ca regivers.  She re viewed the  purpose a nd require ments of the
Community Enhancement Grant Program, noting that Cheyenne does not currently have this type
of playground in the community; advised the project is estimated to cost $420,000.00 with the grant
request in the amount of $210,000.00, and stated the City will match the grant request amount
through in-kind services, community facility fees and a recreation mill grant that has already been
approved for the pr oject.  Ms. S chneider also reviewed the time fra mes involved with the g rant
process.  Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, distributed a project summary sheet (on file
with City Clerk’s off ice); provided a br ief history of ef forts made toward implem entation of a
universally accessible playground, and e xplained why Cahill P ark was selected as the loc ation.
Speaking in support of the playground project were Monica Puente, former Leadership Cheyenne
participant, and JoAnne Weigand and Anne Picot, Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities.
Comments included whether Cahill Park was the best location for the playground.  There being no
further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the
public hearing.
ORDINANCE - A mending Section 6.04.020, Definitions, of Chapter 6.04, Definitions,
Administration and Enforcement; Creating a new Section 6.08.190, Community Cat Initiative, of
Chapter 6.08, Animal Care and Control Regulations Generally; and Amending Section 6.20.020,
Animal Impoundment, of Chapter 6.20, Impoundment, of Title 6, Animals, of the Code of the City
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mayor Kaysen requested Ms. Broyles, as sponsor of the ordinance, provide
an explanation of the purpose of the ordinance.  Ms. Broyles advised the ordinance would allow for
a more effective approach of addressing the community’s current homeless cat population and will
involve a humane  proce ss of trapping , neuter ing, a nd rele asing the cats, resulting  in a lower
population of community cats.  Britney Wallesch, Executive Director, Black Dog Animal Rescue,
spoke in support of the or dinance and use of humane “trap and return” programs.  Reporting for
Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms.
Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
 
ORDINANCE - A mending Section 12 .04.010, Obstructions on Streets, and Sec tion
12.04.080, Construction and Repair - Permits Required - Fees - Exception, of Chapter 12.04, Streets,
Sidewalks and Driveways Generally, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Ms. Broyles, as sponsor of the ordinance, briefly explained the
ordinance would provide for clarification regarding the placing of storage containers and roll-offs
within City rights-of-way and reviewed elements of the proposed ordinance involving a permit
process (with a fee); landscaping projects/materials; time limits, and notification (permit posting)
requirements.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on second reading,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry regarding the proposed 72 hour time limit for obstructions
involving landscaping materials, Rob Geringer, City Engineer, advised the time limit will provide
for proper protection for sediment control and run-off concerns.  He advised this could be construed
as an e nforcement issu e if landscaping obstructions ex tend beyond the time limit .  Discussion
followed on the length of time needed to complete certain landscaping projects.  Dr. Rinne moved
to amend par agraph C.1. of Sec tion 12.04.010 to ins ert the w ords “without a permit” so the
subsection would re ad as f ollows, seconded by  Mr. Cook: “The pe riod of obstruction shall not
exceed seventy-two (72) hours without a permit;”.  Comments followed on the initial intent of the
ordinance, which does not have a permit process applicable to landscaping materials; reasonable time
limit regarding landscaping materials as obstructions, and concerns with displaying a permit if a
permit is required for landscaping materials.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Broyles,
Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor a nd Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Mr. Luna, Mr.
Valdez and Mayor Kaysen. [City Clerk note:  Announcement of voting results reflected the motion
failed, however, upon subsequent review of votes cast (following closure of the meeting), it was
confirmed the motion to amend carried.]  Ms. Broyles moved to amend by substitute (dated Oct. 27,
2014), seconded by Mr. Brown.  Changes contained in the proposed substitute were reviewed and,
upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, clarified language shown as a “strike through” in Section
12.04.080 B in the substitute had a lso been include d in the  initial ordinance.  Motion to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Luna
voting “no”. [City Clerk note: The motion to amend by  substitute ordinance replaced the initial
ordinance, as amended.]  Discussion followed and included comments in support as well a s
opposition to the ordinanc e.  Topics include d: Current City  Code obstructions  in rights-of-way
restrictions; enforcement concerns; permit/fee requirement; length of the proposed ordinance and
various violation sections, and purpose of the ordinance, which is to address how long storage
containers and landscaping materials may be located on streets (public rights-of-way).  Upon inquiry,
Mr. White advised the ordinance structure format was based on an ordinance considered by City
Council a few years ago, and at the current time it is unlawful to have obstructions of this type in the
public rights-of-way.  He explained the ordinance would provide for the obstructions under certain
conditions, and include affiliated violations, and would allow people to use rights-of-way for private
uses on a limited basis.  Motion to approve on second reading, as amended by substitute ordinance,
failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne, and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” --  Mr.
Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.

[CA] RESOLUTION - Re-appropr iating the Fiscal Year 2015 budg et of the City  of Cheyenne
