RESOLUTION - G ranting an exception for de velopment of small waste water treatment
systems pursuant to the 201 Agreement for Cheyenne Power Park, a planned unit development,
located generally within the NW1/4 of Section 29, T.13N., R.66W., of the 6  P.M., Laramie County,th
Wyoming.  Ga ry Hickm an, Chey enne-Laramie County  Hea lth Depar tment, Division of
Environmental Health, briefly explained the 201 Agreement and provisions within the Agreement
for granting an exception to requirements as affiliated with the resolution.  James Perrott McNeil
spoke in opposition to development of any land or any zone changes on Union Pacific (railroad)
property.  Casey  Palma, Steil  Survey ing Ser vices, a gent for the  proper ty owner , ref erenced
information that had been previously provided regarding approval of the hardship exception request
to allow for 11 small septic system permits (one per lot) to be issued for the Cheyenne Power Park,
located south of Cheyenne, for development purposes, and requested approval of the resolution.  Mr.
Shanor reported there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Mr.
Shanor m oved to  a dopt, se conded b y Mr. Luna.  M r. Sha nor m oved to  a mend by  su bstitute
resolution, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  It was advised the proposed substitute addresses concerns that
had been raised relating to “land locking” growth of the City by adding Condition c. under Section
2 of the resolution to read as follows: “If, and when, conditions for annexation exist for any of the
properties governed by the Cheyenne Power Park PUD, the owner or owners of each such property
shall seek a nnexation int o the municipal boundar ies of the City  of Chey enne.”.  Mr. Hickman
explained that whenever permits are issued it includes wor ding that when City  sewer services
become available, the landowner would be required to hook-up.  Discussion followed and included
the following topics: Whether the amendment, the exception, and 201 Agreement generally “runs”
with the property or with the current or future owner of the property; party responsible for acquiring
the State Engineer’s permit to drill water wells; lot sizes relative to septic systems; whether permits
are recorded with the County Clerk; annexation; property covenants; maintenance of 201 permits
through t he Environmental He alth office ; potential scena rios if wells/septic sy stems fail, and
enforcement of the 201 Agreement.  Upon inquiry during discussion, Dan White, City Attorney, and
Mr. Hickman provided information available relating to permits; hardship exemptions; septic system
criteria; whether permits “run” with the prope rty itself or with the prope rty owner, and the 201
Agreement, which deals with septic/sewage systems, not water wells.  Motion to amend car ried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Williams
voting “no”.  Main motion, as amended by substitute, carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present with the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”. (#5623)

The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning

Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held November 24, 2014, at 6:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming to receive public comments
regarding the sale of a tract of land owned by the City of Cheyenne, located at 206 West Lincolnway,
Cheyenne, Wyoming, legally described as the East 22 feet of Lot 7, Block 390, Original City, to the
Children’s Museum of Cheyenne, Inc., a Wyoming corporation, for economic development purposes
pursuant to W.S. § 15-1-112(b)(i)(D), and in exchange for consideration as provided by Section 5
of the Project Development and Administration Agreement entered into between the City and the
Children’s Museum of Cheyenne, Inc., dated October 6, 2014. (City Contract # 6242).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held November 24, 2014, at 6:00 p.m.
in Cit y Coun cil Chambers, 2101 O’ Neil Avenue , Chey enne, Wy oming to dete rmine whe ther
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 ex ist for a parcel of land
situated in a portion of the NW 1/4 of Section 21, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Laramie County,
Wyoming, to be known as The Bluffs, 10th Filing, Annexation (located between Converse Avenue
and Crestridge Drive, north of Foothills Road).
Under other business:
Major Dorr commented on the recent programs that were provided free to the public by the
Laramie County Community College’s Music Department, noting low spectator attendance, and
encouraged people to attend.
Ms. Williams stated that at the October 27, 2014 City Council meeting the motion to approve
an ordinance amending portions of City Code Title 12 regarding Obstructions on Streets failed on
second reading, and, having voted on the prevailing side and pursuant to Chapter 2.04 of City Code,
moved to reconsider the vote pursuant to potential amendments that may be proposed to the
ordinance, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon request by Mayor Kaysen, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk,
read th e e ntitlement o f t he or dinance pr oposed f or re consideration int o the record a s f ollows:
“Ordinance  - 2nd Reading  - Amending Section 12.04.010, Obstruction s on Streets, and Sec tion
12.04.080, Construction and Repair - Permits Required - Fees - Exception, of Chapter 12.04, Streets,
Sidewalks and Driveways Generally, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.”  Mr. Shanor raised a point of order regarding who was eligible
to second a motion to reconsider.  Mayor Kaysen advised any City Council member can second the
motion (per City Code).  Upon query by Mayor Kaysen, Ms. Williams advised during discussion at
the last City Council meeting, suggestions for potential amendments were brought up and purpose
of the motion to recon sider is to enable furthe r discussion involving possible cha nges to the
ordinance.  Motion to reconsider carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne, Ms.
Williams and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Cook, Mr.  Luna and Mr. Shanor.
Mayor Kaysen stated that as the motion to reconsider had carried, the main motion to approve the
ordinance on second reading, as amended by substitute at the October 27th City Council meeting,
was now back before the governing body.  M s. Williams moved to postpone for two weeks (to
November 24, 2014), seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Following comments, motion to postpone carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Mayor Kaysen announced that due to
postponement, the item will be reviewed at the next Finance Committee meeting, which will be held
Monday, November 17, 2014 at 11:00 a.m., Room 122, of the Municipal Building.  
There being no further business to c ome before the governing body, the m eeting was
officially adjourned at 6:25 p.m. 
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 November 20, 2014

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-11-24 | CLIP_ID: 455 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 455

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
November 24, 2014
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at 6:00
p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS - Mr.
Allen (arriving at 6:30 p.m.), Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez  and
Ms. Williams.  Absent:  Mr. Cook.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer,
and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be routine
items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate discussion on
these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other members is
received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal sequence on the
agenda.) Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Shanor and Ms. Broyles,
agenda item #28(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon request by Ms. Broyles, and pursuant
to support received by Mr. Brown and Dr. Rinne, ag enda item #27(b) was removed from the Consent
Agenda.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Ms. Williams.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on November 10, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interes t in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Shanor  moved to approve payment of vouchers
as presented, seconded by Ms. Wi lliams.  Upon inquiry , Rick Parish, Parks and Re creation Director,
provided information on a purchase order payable to Brody Chemical for AquaBlue lake dye (used to
reduce algae growth in lakes), advising the dye is applied at a minimum of once a year.  Motion carried.
Voting “ yes” - - all member s of the g overning body  prese nt in Council C hambers.  Following
announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive public comments regarding the sale of a tract
of land owned by the City of Cheyenne, located at 206 West Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming, legally
described as the East 22 feet of Lot 7, Block 390, Original City, to the Children’s Museum of Cheyenne,
a Wyoming corporation, for economic development purposes pursuant to W.S. § 15-1-112(b)(i)(D), and
in exchange for consideration as provided by Section 5 of the Project Development and Administration
Agreement entered into between the City and the Children’s Museum of Cheyenne, dated October 6, 2014
(City Contract # 6242) ( Rescheduled from  11/10/14).  Aimee D endrinos, Children’s Museum of
Cheyenne, pr ovided information on the Children’ s Museum’s proposed utilization of the land, and
commented on the Museum’s educational aspects, employment opportunities and economic development
benefits resulting from the Museum’s new building project.  Jim Weaver, agent with #1 Properties, spoke
on behalf of Outback Investments, owner of the Hynds Building (adjacent property), expressing support
for the Children’s Museum’s building project but noting the Hynds owner’s concern that discussions on
a corridor between the two buildings for potential joint access and benefit may not be held once the sales
transaction for the prope rty is complete and the  City is no longe r involved.  He discusse d provisions
contained in the previously approved contract (Contract #6242) relative to construction of the Children’s
Museum and potential effect on the Hynds Building, current status of seeking an attorney general’s opinion
on transac tion lega lities as stated in the contra ct, and the c orridor’s importance r elative to sale s
marketability of the Hynds Building.  U pon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised, pursuant to
wording contained in the contract, a letter was submitted to the Wyoming Attorney General’s Office and
a response letter was received indicating the Attorney General would not be rendering an opinion at this
time on the legality of the transaction.  Mr. Weaver questioned whether the entire agreement was still
viable, or if it would be amended, with regard to the Attorney General’s response.  Mr. White declined to
respond to the question, expressing his belief that it would be inappropriate for him to do so at this time
as his response would be more a matter of confidential legal advice to City Council.  Discussion topics
included: General belief that the Museum’s representative(s) intent is to communicate with the owner of
the Hynds Building; time in which the owner of the Hynds Building has had to present a viable offer to
purchase the City’s property, and sale of the property to an interested party (i.e. Children’s Museum) for
less than the appraised value.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Weaver provided information on past presentations that
have been made to the City regarding proposed use of a corridor relative to development of “the hole”,
noting the concept was never to buy and use the property but how to best utilize use of a corridor.  Mayor
Kaysen noted the City has received conceptual proposals in the past but nothing had been requested to
bring a for mal proposal for ward.  There  being no furt her comments or discussion and purpose  of the
hearing having been met, May or Kaysen closed the  public hear ing.  He advised Agenda Item #23, a
resolution affiliated with the proposed sale, was on the current agenda and had been referred (as a Consent
Agenda item) to Finance Committee, which will discuss the resolution at its 11 a.m. meeting on December
1, 2014.
Mayor Ka ysen opene d a public hea ring to rec eive public c omments to determine whether
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land situated
in a portion of the NW 1/4 of Section 21, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be
known as The Bluffs, 10th Filing, Annexation (located between Converse Avenue and Crestridge Drive,
north of Foothills Road).  Logan Graves, Planning and Development office, stated the proposed annexation
is pursuant to a petition requesting annexation submitted by the property owner and that requirements and
compliance of annexation conditions contained in state statutes had been met. Ms. Graves noted zoning
and final plat actions are also involved for consideration and that the preliminary plat is in process.  There
being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed
the public hearing.

ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section, 2.2.1, Zoning  Map Amen dments, Section 5.1.2, Z oning

with the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”. 
ORDINANCE - A mending the Central Business District (CBD) zoning to include the design
standards currently existing as the Small Scale Commercial and Mixed Use Design Standards described
in Cheyenne Unified Development Code Section 6.7.   [City Clerk note: Mr. Allen joined the meeting in
progress.]  Boyd Wiggam, Wyoming Liberty Group, expressed his belief that the or dinance would not
forward downtown e conomic deve lopment effor ts and may  result in unintended consequenc es, and
commented on elements involved in the standards and how they would be applied to certain downtown
properties relative to existing vs. new buildings.  Speaking in favor of the design standards were:  David
Ohde, DDA/Mainstreet Design Committee, commenting in support of the standar ds applying to ne w
construction and additions which will benefit the downtown area; Dick O’Gara, Vice-President, DDA
Board, expressing support for the standards and mandatory guidelines and noting the DDA Board has not
yet had an opportunity to officially vote on the standards, and Glen Garrett, downtown property owner,
citing the standard’s positive impact on property values, quality building, and downtown and community
benefits.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Mr. Shanor moved
to amend to include Staff  Recommenda tion #2 (to ex clude Sec tion 6.7.3.c, Enclosure  Ratio, from
applicability to the Central Business District zone), seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Comments followed on the
reason for the pr oposed amendment.  Motion to amend car ried.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the
governing body present.  Upon inquiry regarding the standards’ applicability to additions and whether it
would also apply to exi sting buildings affiliated with an addition, Br andon Cammarata, Planning a nd
Development Director, advised the requirements would apply to new additions to an existing building.
Discussion followed on setbacks, lot lines and what c onstitutes an addition.  Mr. Cammara ta provided
information pertaining to the topics raised, and clarified interpretation of an addition would be based upon
applicant information submitted through the building permit process.  Further comments followed on the
need for guidelines for new construction downtown; past efforts to implement standards specifically
patterned for downtown; waiting to approve any standards following review of regulatory processes to be
more business fr iendly, including a dditional public input, and after de termining the effects of
implementation of the downtown facade program and the DDA/Main Street loan program, and Planning
Commission recommendations related to the topic.  Main motion as amended  failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne, and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr.
Valdez and Ms. Williams.
ORDINANCE - Amending Section 12.04.010, Obstructions on Streets, and Sec tion 12.04.080,
Construction and Repair - Permits Required - Fees - Exception, of Chapter 12.04, Streets, Sidewalks and
Driveways Generally, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks a nd Public Pl aces, of the Code of the  City of
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Pursuant to recognition by the chair (i.e. Mayor Kaysen), Ms. Broyles requested
the opportunity to make a statement in conjunction with a request she would be making to withdraw the
item from the agenda.  Mr. Shanor raised a point of order questioning whether comments could be made
prior to having  a motion on the floor.  May or Kaysen responded that he w ould offer Ms. Broyles, as
sponsor of the or dinance, the opportunity to comment rela tive to her intention to reque st the item be
withdrawn from the agenda.  Ms. Broyles commented on the initial purpose of the ordinance and, noting
there had bee n numerous discussions on the topic and that membe rs of the gov erning body have not
reached a consensus of agreement on what should be done to address obstructions on streets, requested
the item be withdrawn from the agenda.  Following query to members of the governing body present and
with no objection being raised, Mayor Kaysen announced the item was being withdrawn from the agenda.

recommendation to approve).
Preliminary Plat, a replat of a portion of Section 35, T.14N., R.66W. of the 6th P.M., Cheyenne,
Laramie County, Wyoming (located east of Pennsylvania Avenue, extending  both 13  Street and Atlanticth
Drive).  L ee Ma rtin, speaking  on behalf  of the owne r/applicant, review ed the a rea proposed f or
development, affiliated actions involving the Preliminary Plat and overall concept of a proposed housing
development consisting of 37 residential homes.  He advised they are aware of concerns regarding traffic
and dr ainage.  M r. Sha nor m oved to  a cknowledge the  Pla nning Com mission’s r ecommendation to
approve, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Discussion followed on drainage concerns relative to changes in the
water table, and traffic concerns, including limited exit areas from the subdivision.  Upon inquiry, Kelly
Hafner, Benchmark Engineers, provided information on water level testing that will be conducte d and
possibility of not including basements in some of the homes to be built, and Mr. Martin confirmed they
will not be “raising the streets” in conjunction with the development project.  Mike Gostovich, W2 Traffic
Engineering, expressed his belief that the development will not pose a significant traffic impact.  Mayor
Kaysen advised there will be an updated traffic study conducted.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”.
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Assured Equity Management
Corporation for health i nsurance consulting services (Contingency Funds a nd Unrestricted Reserves).
Rich Wiederspahn, Human Resources Director, reviewed the purpose of the proposed agreement, which
is to receive professional assistance in reviewing short and long term employee health plan objectives,
evaluating providers and plans offered, ensuring compliance with federal regulations, suggesting cost
reduction ideas and efficiencies, and working with employees for input on plan benefits.  Mr. Wiederspahn
explained the Request for Proposal (RFP) process that was utilized, number of responding vendors, vendor
interviews and resulting recommendation of a one  year agreement with Assured Equity  Management
Corporation by the review committee.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve
in an amount not to exceed $90,000.00, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Discussion topics included:  Concerns
regarding the funding amount and use of reserves; whether the services could be provided by in-house
staff; whether i nformation was available regarding Assured Equity’s history of providing savings to
clients; scope of services to be  provided; issuing an RFP for health plan proposals an d utiliz ing
professional services to review proposals; and employee wellness program status.  Upon inquiry, Josh
Feiman, Vice-President, Assured Equity Management Corporation, provided information on ser vices
provided by the company that include helping businesses evaluate providers and health plans; dealing with
insurance complexities; reviewing and monitoring c osts, and communicating  with em ployees on pla n
elements.  Mr. Feiman stated the company’s proposal was submitted per the guidelines requested by the
City, and indicated he could provide examples of savings the firm had provided for other clients in the
region.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Wiederspahn advised the insurance committee and staff are working on an
alternative employee wellness program which would be voluntary for participation.  Dr. Rinne moved to
postpone to December 8, 2014, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” --
Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr.
Brown, Mr. Luna and Mr . Valdez.  Upon inquiry  from Mayor Kaysen for clarification purposes, Mr.
Wiederspahn a nd Patti Garule, Human Resour ces of fice, provided brie f informa tion reg arding the
insurance committee’s involvement regarding the RFP.

Powers Board is also a contract participant.]

Appointments of Landon Brown, Philip J. Griggs and Kenneth C. Uhrich, to the City Planning

Ms. Williams moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members

annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land situated in the S½ of Lot
4, Williams Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at 812 Storey Boulevard).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held December 8, 2014 at 6:00 P.M. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’N eil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether compliance of
annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land situated in a portion of
Tracts 1 & 7 and all of Tracts 26 & 27, Dell Range Addition; Lots 1 & 2 of Smithson View, Laramie
County, Wyoming (located between Ridge Road and College Drive along Thomas Road).
Announcement was made of a Public Meeting to be held Monday, December 8, 2014 at 6:00 P.M.
in  City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the purpose of receiving public
comment on the submission of a 5307 Federal Grant to fund transportation services by Cheyenne Transit
for Fiscal Year 2015. 
Announcement was made of the  Re-Appointment of Kevin Paintner to the Chey enne Regional
Airport Board, to be confirmed December 8, 2014. 
Under other business:
Due to timeliness involving the pr oject, a Contract Agreement between the City of Cheyenne,
Parks and Recreation Department, and Public Restroom Company, Minden, Nevada for the provision and
installation of a restroom facility for Romero Park (Community Development Block Grant Funds) was
introduced and referred to Finance Committee.
Mr. Shanor inquired about the status of the Planning and Development office changes pursuant to
the recent resolution adopted in which the development office would be structured as a department, and
requested, pursuant to a recent City Council work session (re: Capital Improvement Plan presentation),
whether staff could internally conduct an impact study regarding regional fees in lieu of a potential change
order.  Mayor Kaysen advised discussions are in progress regarding the Development department relative
to budget coordination, and that the impact study topic had been taken under advisement after the work
session and discussions are continuing.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 7:54  p.m. 
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2014-12-08 | CLIP_ID: 457 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 457

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
December 8, 2014
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr.
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and
Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Shanor and Dr. Rinne, agenda item #19 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Ms. Williams
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on November 24, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
 Mr. Valdez  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Shanor.
Upon inquiry, Lois Huff, City  Treasurer, provided infor mation regarding vouchers payable to:
Lincoln Financial Groups (pa yroll deduction payments for e mployees who have pur chased life
insurance); Employers Council Services, Inc. (services for investigating certain personnel issues);
Evergreen Lawn and Tree Care, Inc. (tree removal/pruning assistance for a qualifying individual
under the Community Development Block Grant HOME program), and Paddle Wheeler (purchase
of paddle boats (Lions Park) involving L aramie County Recreation Board grant funds).  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Mayor Kaysen opened a pu blic hearing to de termine whether compliance of annexation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land situated in the S½ of Lot 4,
Williams Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at 812 Storey Boulevard).  Josh Tetzlaff,
Planning a nd Deve lopment office , advised the pr oposed annexation is pursuant to a petition
requesting annexation submitted by the property owner; briefly reviewed the area to be annexed, and
stated that requirements and compliance of annexation conditions contained in state statutes had
been met.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met,
Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a publi c hearing  to determine w hether compliance of annexation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land situated in a portion of Tracts
1 & 7 and all of Tracts 26 & 27, Dell Range Addition; Lots 1 &  2 o f Smithson View, Laramie
County, Wyoming (located between Ridge Road and College Drive along Thomas Road).  Josh
Tetzlaff, Planning and Development office, advised the proposed annexation is pursuant to a petition
requesting annexation submitted by the property owners; briefly reviewed the area to be annexed,
and stated that requirements and compliance of annexation conditions contained in state statutes had
been met.  Mr. Tetzlaff noted a zone change and platting actions are in process.  Kay McAdams
questioned whether Lot 5, Block 8 would be included in the area to be annexed and applicability of
a proposed zone change to AR (Agricultural Residential) for the lot.  Mr. Tetzlaff verified the lot was
included in the propose d annexation are a and  Jim Flesher , Planning a nd Deve lopment office ,
confirmed an AR zone is a C ity zone classification.  Mayor Kaysen advised agenda item no. 16
pertains to the zone change proposed for the property.  D. J. Perrott Macneil commented generally
in opposition to annexations an d zone changes involving privately leased lands and submitted a
handout (on file with City Clerk’s office) in conjunction with his statements.  Mr. Brown raised a
point of order that Mr. Macneil’s comments were not specific to the property involving the public
hearing with Mayor Kaysen requesting Mr. Macneil to summarize his comments as related to the
topic on the floor.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been
met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public meeting for the purpose of receiving public comment on the
submission of a 5307 Federal Grant to fund transportation services by Cheyenne Transit for Fiscal
Year 2015.   Joe Dougherty, Transit Director, provided information regarding the grant submission
requesting $1,542,855 in F ederal assistance  for c apital equipmen t, maintenance and ope rating
expenses, and funds received from other sources for a gross project total of $2,554,151.  In addition,
Mr. Dougherty briefly reviewed transit services provided throughout the past year and, upon inquiry,
advised of anticipated FY2016 budget funding requests for new buses and capital expenditures,
indicating the program is subsidized by the general fund in the approximate amount of $400,000-
$500,000.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met,
Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section, 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from AG Agricultural to HI Heavy Industrial for Lot 1 &
Lot 2, Block 1 of Plains Industrial Park, 2  Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southwestnd
corner of the intersection of Campstool Road and HR Ranch Road).  Reporting for Public Services
Committee, Mr. Sha nor moved to approve  on third and final r eading with the condition that
information r elating to D EQ tr ansportation r egulations a nd ind ustry sta ndards b e su bmitted to
members of the g overning body by the deve loper prior to the Dec ember 8, 2014 meeting  of the
governing body, seconded by Mr. L una.  I t was noted that since  the Commit tee mee ting, the
condition had been met by the developer.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of  the
governing body with the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”.  (#4047)
ORDINANCE - Amending Section 10.32.030, Decreasing State Speed Limits in Ce rtain
Zones, and a mending the attac hed S chedule A-2, of Cha pter 10.32, Spee d Limits, of Title 10,
Vehicles and Tra ffic, of the Code of the City  of Chey enne, Wyoming.  Er nie Valdez expressed
concerns with the speed limits around Goins Elementary school, stating his belief that the limits need
to be reduced and flashing lights should be installed around the school for safety purposes.  Mr.
Shanor moved to appr ove on sec ond reading, seconded by Ms. Wi lliams.  Upon inquiry , Rob
Geringer, City Engineer, reviewed language contained in the existing ordinance and Schedule A-2
that includes wording indicating the 25 mph speed limit i s effective in areas when properly sign-
posted, and explained that since that ordinance change was approved (2004) an issue was ra ised
regarding signage and where it had to be posted.  He stated by striking out the wording, pursuant to
the proposed ordinance, signage could be posted in the same locations as the handheld cellphone
ordinance notification signs (i.e. entry streets into the City) indicating the speed limit on local streets
is 25 mph unless otherwise posted.  He explained this would assist with enforcement efforts, which
is the overall intent of the proposed amendment.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Geringer and Captain Mitch
Maxwell, Police Department, described public education efforts and related officer enforcement plan
that will be made to advise the public of the City-wide change if the ordinance is approved.  Mr.
Geringer, in response to inquiry, also provided information generally on the street map adopted by
the City relative to residential vs. collector and/or arterial streets.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion of the NW1/4 of
Section 21, T.14N., R.66W., 6  P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be known as The Bluffs, 10th th
Filing Annexation (located between Converse Avenue and Crestridge Drive, north of Foothills
Road).  Mr. Shanor  moved to approve  on second r eading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Amending Chapter 13.12, Sewer System, of Title 13, Public Services, of the
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, by revising Section 13.12.030, Deposit of Excrement,
Garbage or O bjectionable Waste Prohibited, and a dding Sec tion 13.12.195, Unauthorized and
Prohibited Discharges.  Clint Bassett, Board of Public Utilities, briefly reviewed the intent of the
ordinance, which is to inform  c ustomers of items that cause  obstructions, damag es and se wer
backups if discharged into the sewer system.   He noted the ordinance will also protect employees
and water treatment processes.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms.
Williams.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Bassett stated applicable sections in the ordinance will assist in
identifying violators, and that customers will be informed of the items that may damage the system.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the S1/2 of Lot 4, Williams
Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at 812 Storey Boulevard) -- referred to Public
Services Committee (sponsor -- Mr. Shanor).

After the next agenda item title was read into the record, Mayor Kaysen explained, for benefit
of the public in attendance, the above ordinances introduced on first reading were referral items to

on December 16, 2014 at Noon in Room 122.
RESOLUTION - An exception to the “201 Agreement” to allow a septic permit for a small
wastewater treatment system for a  residential pr operty for the south 1/2 of Tr acts 105 & 106,
Sunnyside Addition, 4th Filing, Chey enne, Wy oming ( located at the northea st corner  of the
intersection of Monroe Ave nue and Cha rles Stree t).  Cooper Ove rstreet, The Kuke r Group,
representing the property owners, reviewed the history of the property that had been annexed into
the City based upon the owners’ belief that City water and sewer services were available near the
property.  Subsequently, he advised, it was discovered that sewer and water lines were not in the
vicinity, therefore, the owners submitted an application for a septic permit as an exception to the
“201 Agreement” to allow for any needed repairs to their system.  He stated the Health Department
denied the permit and if the governing body does not approve the exception, the property owners will
pursue de-annexation of the prope rty.  Roy  Kroeger, City/County Environmental Health office,
provided information and reviewed reasons for denial of the permit request.  Mr. Shanor reported
there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Mr. Shanor moved to
adopt, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Discussion followed on the “201 Agreement”, which does not allow
the City to grant an exception for properties within the City;  circumstances involving the subject
property, and possibility of de-annexing the property.  Motion failed.  Voting “no” -- all members
of the governing body.
 [CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to lease, with an option to
purchase, sanitation, recycling, and landfill equipment (Solid Waste Fund).  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried. (#5631)
RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sell a tract of land owned by the
City of Cheyenne, located at 206 West Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and having an appraised
value of seventy thousand dollars ($70,000.00), to the Children’s Museum of Cheyenne, a Wyoming
Corporation, subject to the payment of  the pu rchase price of one hundred dollars ($100.00), and
subject to the terms and c onditions of the Project Deve lopment and Administration Ag reement
entered into by the City of Cheyenne and the Children’s Museum of Cheyenne dated October 6, 2014
(City Contract # 6242).  Amy Surdam, Board President, Children’s Museum, explained the mission
of the Children’s Muse um and its project inv olving the building of  a lea rning and educ ational
children’s museum, and its relationship with downtown redevelopment.  She noted the importance
of acquiring the property to move forward with the project and fund-raising and grant opportunities;
advised of the  status of conc eptual and sc hematic building  desig ns, and commented on
communications involving the Hynds Building as an adjacent property.  Jim Weaver, #1 Properties,
speaking on behalf of the owner (Outback Investments, LLC) of the Hynds Building, commented
in support of the Children’s Museum project generally but requested a two week postponement of
the agenda item to allow further discussions between the Children’s Museum and Hynds Building
owner regarding a possible memorandum of understanding relative to joint utilization of corridor
space which would be of benefit to both owners.  Ms. Surdam spoke in opposition to postponement
due to its effect on the Museum’s ability to apply for a National Endowment for the Arts grant by
the grant deadline, and other pending grant and donation opportunities based upon ownership of the
property.  Pat Crank, attorney representing the Children’s Museum, referenced and commented on
a re cent le tter t hat ha d b een is sued b y the  ow ner o f t he Hynds B uilding ind icating int erest i n
acquiring the City’s property for the appraised value; r eviewed past communication ef forts the
Museum has made with the owner of the Hynds Building; advised they are willing to sit down with
Outback Investments to work on issues, and spoke  in oppositi on to postp oning action on the
resolution.  Mr. Weaver  provided re asons for O utback’s offer to purcha se the prope rty, and the
necessity for an agreement with whomever may own the 22 ft. of property. Reporting for Finance
Committee, Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Shanor  moved to amend by
adding the following “Whereas” clause to the resolution, seconded by Mr. Allen:  “Whereas, this
body explicitly finds that the sale of the specified land at less than appraised value will promote
economic development in Cheyenne in a  variety of ways.”  To m Johnson, Wyoming Business
Council, advised the Museum’s pr oposed building pr oject i s consistent with simi lar ty pes of
economic development projects and that a study and analysis had been done relative to direct and
indirect economic development benefits.  He stated the B usiness Council would consider a g rant
request from the Museum with or without “Hynds involvement”, and noted that the Attorney General
has provided [legal] guidance on similar past projects.  In response to query by Mr. Weaver, Mr.
Johnson advised the economic study did not include the Hynds building.  Discussion on the proposed
amendment followed with Mr. Weaver providing information in response to questions involving
current use of a  portio n of the Hy nds building, plans for  rede velopment of the building , and
relationship of a joint corridor between the Hynds and future Museum building.  Motion to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Discussion on the main motion, as
amended, included the following topics: Wording contained in City Contract #6242 regarding an
Attorney General’s opinion; Hynds building owner concerns; National Endowment for the Arts grant
deadline, Children’s Museum project as a downtown asset, and grant involvement by the City.
During discussion and upon inquiry, Mr. Crank stated his interpretation of City Contract wording
regarding an Attorney General’s opinion, and related state laws; Mr. Weaver expressed that if the
property ownership is transferr ed and th e City is no longe r involved there  is a conc ern that the
incentive in reaching an agreement (re: joint corridor) will be lessened, and Ms. Surdam provided
information on the Arts grant deadline.  Main motion, as amended, carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Brown voting “no”. (#5632)
 
[CA] RESOLUTION - Acknowledging the initiation and development of the Cheyenne Historic
Façade Improvement Program for Downtown Cheyenne.  Motion (per Consent Ag enda) carried.
(#5633)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Acknowledging receipt of the Cheyenne Cemetery Arboretum Master Plan
Report and Appendices, dated September 15, 2014, prepared by Robert Peccia and Associates.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5634)
RESOLUTION - Establishing guidelines for the maximum amount of excess revenue that
may be collected for the operation of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fund.  Boyd Wiggam, Wyoming
Liberty Group, sta ted th e resolution po ses a  ph ilosophical de cision on  the  iss ue of  wh ether t o
continue to rely on sanitation fees to fund other City operations, noting limited revenue sources for
the City and elements of an enterprise fund.  Ed Strader and Ed Melko commented in support of the
resolution, noting f unding needs for the landfill and the ne ed for the City  to find other revenue
sources or reduce expenditures.  Mr. Melko’s comments included reference to the use of a portion
of sanitation set-aside funds to purchase the Belvoir Ranch potentially to utilize as a new landfill site,
and suggesting the ranch, or portion thereof, be sold to raise funds.  Donald James Perrott Macneil
expressed his belief that the City does not own the Belvoir Ranch.  During Mr. Macneil’s comments,
Mr. Brown raised a point of order that comments were not applicable to the resolution topic with
Mayor Kaysen advising Mr. Mac neil that his statements neede d to stay  on subject.  Mr. B rown
reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Shanor moved
to adopt, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Comment followed on the results of the recent sanitation study and
affiliated projected fee increases; amount of funds transferred to the general fund, and purpose of the
resolution.  Ms. Broyles moved to amend the 5th line of the last paragraph of the resolution that
begins with “Now, Therefore, Be It Resolved” by striking “five percent (5%)” and replacing it with
“nine percent (9%)”, and stated the rest of the paragraph would remain unchanged and if a second
to her motion is received she would like to explain the reasoning for the amendment.  Mr. Shanor
seconded the motion to amend.  Ms. Broyles explained her reasons for proposing the amendment.
Mr. Strader  spoke in opposition to t he ame ndment, and Mr. Melko questioned the impact on
sanitation rate increases based upon the proposed amendment.  Discussion included the following
topics: Retaining money in the sanitation fund; keeping sanitation rate increases at minimal amounts;
future effect of the percentage change proposed in the amendment, and whether the general fund will
be jeopardized.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen advised it was hard to determine the impact on the
general fund as the next fiscal year budget has not yet been prepared or submitted to the governing
body.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr.
Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Dr. Rinne, Mr. Va ldez and Mayor Kaysen.
Discussion on the main motion, as amended, included the following topics: Justifying rate increases;
future e ffect of the re solution; City  opera tions and service s that do not ge nerate re venue a nd
resolution effect on various departments and operations; potential future changes to the resolution
if needed; concern with limiting the amount of funds tha t can be  transferred; interpretation of
wording in the “Now, T herefore, Be It Resolved” cla use of the r esolution, as amende d; binding
future City Council members with provisions of the resolution; boom and bust revenue cycles;
potentially cutting non- essential services to make  up the funding  difference to the g eneral fund;
implementing parameters for the administration prior to preparation of the next budget, and use of
sanitation fees for the general fund.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen indicated, to t he best of his
knowledge, the practice of using sanitation fees to meet obligations for City-wide services has been
in place for decades, and that utilization of fees for that purpose is not an unc ommon practice by
other cities.  Main motion, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook,
Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Dr. Rinne and
Mayor Kaysen. (#5635)

Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Assured E quity
Management Corpora tion for he alth insurance  consulting se rvices (Continge ncy Funds a nd
Unrestricted Reserves).  Josh Feinman, Vice-President, Assured Equity Management Corporation,
provided a  detailed r eview of  the vari ous types of se rvices that would be provided unde r the
agreement in response to the RF P (Request For  Proposals) the City  had issu ed, and noted
information on examples of  similar services provided by Assured Equity for other clients, as
requested, had been submitted.  Mr. Brown reported there was no recommendation on the item from
Finance Committee.  Ms. Broyles moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Discussion topics
included: whether in-house staff could provide the services; concern with the cost of services to be
provided unde r the one -year ag reement; ag reement term;  health plan c hanges re lated to the
Affordable Care Act; potentially revising the RFP to “downsize” services requested to reduce the
City’s cost, and othe r RFP re sponses rec eived.  Dur ing disc ussion and  upon inquiry , Rich
Wiederspahn, Human Resources Director, clarified the agreement is for professional services and
is not a study; explained in-house City staff are not medical insurance experts, noting other large
organizations have consultant assistance for these areas, and provided information on the RFP
process that was utilized.  He  revie wed RF P proposals rec eived, inter views conduc ted and
recommendation of Assured Equity by  members of the review committee, whose recommendation
was based upon the belief that Assured Equity could meet all elements of the RFP and was not based
upon the price proposed for services.  Motion to approve failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” --
Mr. Allen, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Mr.
Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.

(#6162)

to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Consideration of B id #M-9-15 for  a contr act be tween the  City  of Chey enne, F orestry
Division, and Western Tree Care, Inc., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the City of Cheyenne Cemetery
Complex Tree Removal project -- referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M -10-15 fo r a  c ontract be tween th e City of  Che yenne, Landfill
Division, and J-2 Contracting Company, Greeley, Colorado, for crushing concrete at the Happy Jack
Landfill (Solid Waste Management) -- referred to Finance Committee. 
Re-Appointment of Kevin Paintner to the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board.  Mr. Shanor
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion car ried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body  with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointee). 
[CA] Consideration  to purchase nine Motorola radios for new police vehicles for the Cheyenne
Police Department from Absolute Solutions, Chey enne, Wyoming (2011 - 2014 1%  Sales Tax).
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $35,662.50).
Announcement was made of the Re-Appointments of Paul M. Frauendienst to the Building
Code Board of Appeals, Mark C. Hardee to the Fire Civil Service Commission, and Tony Adams
to the Personnel Commission, to be confirmed December 22, 2014. 
Under other business, Andy Austin expressed concerns regarding the fee assessed to residents
for parking permits associated with the recently approved Residential Critical Traffic and Parking
Area in the vicinity of the Cheyenne Regional Medical Center.  He expressed appreciation for the
City taking action to designate the area but noted his frustration with having to pay for a permit(s)
and suggested at least one permit be allowed at no charge.  Brief discussion followed on permit fee
requirements per the Critical Traffic and Parking Area ordinance, and possible modification to the
ordinance and potential effect on other Critical Traffic and Parking Areas that have been approved
in the past re lative to annual par king permits.  During  discussion Carol I ntlekofer, City Clerk,
confirmed that ag enda doc umentation submitt ed with the applica tion for g overning body
consideration to establish the area included a copy of the Critical Traffic and Parking Area ordinance
(i.e. City Code) and that the application form requesting the area contains a requirement that the
applicant must notify affected residents of the parking permit requirement.
There being  no fur ther business to come be fore the g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 9:29 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 December 19, 2014

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
December 22, 2014
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook (arriving at 6:13 p.m.), Mr. Luna, Dr.
Rinne, Mr. Shanor (a rriving at 6:03 p.m.), Mr. Va ldez and Ms. Wi lliams.  Also present: Carol
Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledg e of
allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed  from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Allen, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Brown and Mr. Luna, agenda item #18 was re moved from the Consent Ag enda.  Mr. Allen
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. [Mr. Shanor joined the meeting in
progress]
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 8, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Service s) and va cated Co uncil Chambers.  Mr. V aldez moved to approve  payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Upon inquiry, Lois Huff, City Treasurer, provided
information available regarding vouchers payable to Action Ads, Inc. (Transit program business card
holders a nd c oin p urses) a nd A ll T raffic D ata Se rvices, I nc. ( traffic c ounts f or M etropolitan
Transportation Organization (MPO)).  Pr oviding additional information on the voucher s under
discussion were Joe Dougherty, Cheyenne Transit Program Director, explaining that the purchases
from Action Ads were for program marketing and passenger use purposes, and Mayor Kaysen, who
advised traffic counts for the MPO were conducted at various locations throughout the community.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.

practice of the governing body is to not postpone an item for more than two weeks at a time (or until
the next regularly scheduled meeting).  Comment was made regarding a map edit change that should
be made prior to final re ading.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the

Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Unified Development Code,
changing t he z oning cl assification fr om HR  Hi gh De nsity R esidential (C ounty) t o HR -2 Hi gh
Density Residential-Developing for a re plat of portions of Tra cts 245, 246, 247, and 248, a nd a
portion of the vacated east half of Arthur Road, Sunnyside Addition, 6th Filing, C heyenne,

ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion of Tracts 1 and 7
and all of Tracts 26 and 27, Dell Range Addition; Lots 1 and 2, Smithson View, Laramie County,
Wyoming, to be known as Thomas Heights Annexation (located between Ridge Road and College
Drive along Thomas Road).  Speaking in opposition to annexation and the affiliated re-zoning and
final plat as pr oposed we re B rien McAd ams, Gail Sundell, Kay  McAda ms and Rob Courtier.
Concerns expressed included: Planning for sidewalks along North College Drive; property setbacks;
traffic flow and safety; ingress/egress into the proposed residential subdivision to be built; reasonable
measures to contain construction debris; proposed zoning classification for Lot 5, Block 8; effect of
the housing development on aesthetic views; Unified Development Code requirements allowing for
a cash-in-lieu payment by the deve loper vs. providing  open g reen space or a pa rk; quantity of
residential lots to be built; pump st ation location; water  levels in the ar ea and drainage run-off;
opposition to development in the area generally; planning for quality developments and responsible
growth; appropriate buffers, and balancing growth between city and county boundaries.  Speaking
in favor of annexation and the proposed residential development project generally were:  Jerry Ciz;
Joe Patterson, President, Southeast Wyoming Homebuilders Association, and Jamie Ciz .  Topics
discussed included: Need for additional residential housing in the community; current number of
buildable lots available within the City and local housing market; developer (Guardian Homes) plans
and conc essions made for  the subdivision, and are a g rowth, includi ng other re cent r esidential
subdivisions that have been developed expanding City limits.  Upon inquiry during comments, Brad
Emmons, AVI, agen t for the applicant (G uardian Homes), pr ovided information on deve loper
concessions for the project and changes that have been made since the initial proposal.  Mr. Allen
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Discussion included the request
for annexation that was submitted by the property owners and conditions for annexation that have
been met, and i nformation on the Sny der Plaza deve lopment topic that had bee n raised dur ing
speaker comments.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Unified Development Code,
changing the zoning classification from A-1 (County), Agricultural and Rural Residential, to AR,
Agricultural Residential, for Lot 5, Block 8, Thomas Heights, and from A-1 (County), Agricultural
and Rur al Re sidential, a nd AR  (C ounty) A gricultural Re sidential, t o M R-2, Me dium-Density
Residential-Developing, for the r emainder of Thomas He ights, Chey enne, Wy oming ( located
between Ridge  Road and College Dr ive along  Thomas Road).  Spea king in opposition to the
ordinance were: Brien McAdams, Kay McAdams and Rob C ourtier.  T opics included: Specific
opposition to the proposed zoning classification for Lot 5, Block 8 and rationale in proposing an AR
zone for the lot; allowable uses in an AR zone, and concerns with zoning laws and encroachment.
Upon inquiry during comments, Brad Emmons, AVI, agent for the applicant, explained why the
property owners of Lot 5, Block 8 r equested an AR zone classification for the lot, and Br andon
Cammarata, Planning a nd Deve lopment Direc tor, provided infor mation conce rning L ot 5 and
allowable uses within an AR zone.  Mr. Allen moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms.
Williams.  Di scussion to pics in cluded: D efinition of  the t erm “limited a griculture” re lative to
interpretation of the Unified Development Code and allowable uses.  During comment regarding
maintenance responsibility of a sidewalk if same were to be built on the east side of the development
project (adjacent to North College Road), Mr. Allen raised a point of order that the inquiry was not
applicable to the motion on the floor (i.e. zoning topic) and was more applicable to discussion on
the plat.  May or Kaysen concurred and re quested comments rema in on topic.  Motion carrie d.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Mayor Kaysen advised the ordinance would be
discussed again at the next Public Services Committee meeting, which will meet January 7, 2015
at noon in Room 122, Municipal Building.
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the filing of an application w ith the  F ederal Transit
Administration, an Operating Administration of the United States Department of Transportation, for
Federal Transportation Assistance authorized by Title 49, United States Code, Section 5307.  Motion
(per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5636)
[CA] RESOLUTION - A uthorizing the  Ma yor a nd City  Clerk to e xecute a  Sta te Hi storic
Preservation Office Certified Local Government grant agreement for attendance at the 2015 Saving
Places Conference.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5637)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Designating local banks as depositories for municipal funds.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried. (#5638)
RESOLUTION - Re- appropriating the Fisca l Year 2015 budg et of the City  of Cheyenne
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113.  Sara Vasquez, President, Cheyenne
Public Employees Association, and Jon Bertsch, President, International Association of Firefighters
Local #279, commented in support of the one-time employee recognition payment included in the
re-appropriation, a nd e xpressed appreciation to  me mbers of  the  g overning bo dy fo r t heir
consideration of the payment.  Re porting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to adopt,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Discussion included the following topics: Timing of discussions by the
administration involving the proposed employee recognition payment; mixed message involving the
payment and recent comments involving possible lay-offs; payment to be taken from reserves where
prior year budget savings were placed; status of the Employee Investment Study (EIS) and pending
court decision; suggestion that a merit-based employee reward program be considered; employee
recognition and the need to achieve median market salary value for positions; providing for funding
of e mployee sa lary re cognitions d uring the budget pr ocess, a nd the  po sitive c urrent e conomic
environment.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Vasquez reviewed the timeframe when the employee payment was
last discussed by the CPEA Board, and her recollection that initial discussion on the funds being set
aside for the payment was in August, prior to recent discussions concerning possible lay-offs.  In
response to queries, Mayor Kaysen advised approximately $1.2 million in budget savings, which had
been placed in reserves, was achieved through employee efforts to curtail expenditures for FY2013-
2014; noted the savings were not intended to go toward implementation of the EIS plan but the intent
is to provide employee recognition of efforts toward limiting expenditures during the last budget
year; reviewed discussions held and a nalysis of the budget conducted resulting in the propose d
payment in an estimated total amou nt of $325,000 fr om unencumber ed budg et re serves, and
explained the criteria employees must meet to receive the maximum payment amount of $500.  Upon
inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, provided an update on the EIS, stating the lawsuit that had been
filed by the Wyoming Tribune-Eagle is still pending and that attorneys for both sides had submitted
additional briefs in November as ordered by the District Court Judge.  During comment regarding
why the EIS was still i n litigation, Mr. B rown raised a point of orde r that the EIS study was not
related to the proposed resolution.  Mayor Kaysen concurred with the point of order with Mr. Shanor
voicing his objection to sustaining the point of order.  Mayor Kaysen stated status of the EIS is under
the control of the court, and, upon inquiry, provided information on the rationale for the employee
recognition payment and his views of a merit-based employee program.  Discussion continued on
the re cognition pay ment, rec ent discussions invol ving future budg et funding  conce rns, and
suggestion of developing a merit-based employee reward program.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body. (#5639)

Announcement was made that the Sine Die meeting of the Governing Body will be held at
2:30 p.m., and the Fir st Regular Meeting of the Gover ning Body will be held at 2:45 p.m., on
Monday, January 5, 2015 in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.
Announcement was made that due to the Sine Die meeting of the Governing Body and the
First Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on Monday, January 5, 2015, regularly scheduled City
Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the Municipal Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue,
Room 122, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance Committee will meet Tuesday, January 6, 2015
at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee will meet Wednesday, January 7, 2015 at Noon.
Under other business, Mayor Kaysen submitted and read a written veto message (on file with
City Clerk’s office) detailing his objections to Resolution No. 5635, adopted by majority vote at the
December 8, 2014 meeting of the governing body. [City Clerk Note: Resolution No. 5635 is titled
as follows: Resolution - Establishing g uidelines for the maximum amount of excess revenue that
may be collected for the operation of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fund.]  Contained in Mayor Kaysen’s
reading of his veto message were references to mayoral veto authority provisions and procedures
contained in Chapter 2.04 of City Code, and provisions of Wyo. Stat. § 16-4-104 (b), Preparation
of budgets; contents; review.  Upon procedural inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised of City
Council members’ process and options to act, or not act, upon the mayor’s veto.  Mr. Shanor moved
to override the mayor’s veto, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Discussion followed on the concern with
lack of prior notice to City Council members regarding the veto action, and potentially postponing
action on the veto to vet information that Mayor Kaysen presented in his statements, particularly
with regard to state statute budget provisions; how a potential postponement will involve newly
elected Council members joining the governing body membership at the next regular meeting, and,
if the motion were to be postponed, who would be responsible for “vetting” the information.  In
response to the timing of submittal of his written veto and objections, Mayor Kaysen explained his
veto took considerable time to deliberate and prepare.  Mr. Allen moved to postpone the motion to
override the veto to January 12, 2015 (next regular meeting of the governing body), seconded by Mr.
Brown.  Discussion included comments in support as well as non-support of postponing the motion.
Motion to postpone failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Mr.
Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Broyles, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor and Ms.
Williams.  Comments followed on recognizing the “will of a large majority of the Council”.  It was
noted that pursuant to state statute, a mayor does not have the authority to vote on their own veto.
Motion to override the Mayor’s veto carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr.
Luna, Mr. Shanor , Mr. Valde z and Ms. W illiams.  Voting “ no” -- Mr. B rown and Dr. Rinne.
Pursuant to a point of order raised by Mr. Allen re garding the number of  votes requir ed for the
motion to carry, it was verified that a minimum 2/3rds affirmative vote of City Council members (i.e.
9 members) was required.
There being  no further  business  to come before the g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 8:15 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
